光 子 分 析 設 備 等 多動能 齊
發 ， 預期 20 2 7 年 營運 明顯
走升  
 汎銓表示今年每月營運成本大致穩定在約 2.1 億元內，27 年每月營運成本約
2.16 億元，只要營收持續成長，獲利將迅速放大。今年 3 月起各個新建廠區已
開始貢獻業績，公司運營已從 3 月開始轉虧為盈。為因應 AI 需求，AI 專區第
三期擴產於 7 月完成並開始貢獻營收，且計畫在年底前開始第四期擴產 ，對下
半年業務樂觀看待，尤其海外業績成長極為迅速，預計到今年底，海外業務佔
比將有望達將近 40%。投顧預期汎銓 4Q26 營收季增 8.6%，年增 39.2%，估
計 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.8 元。 
另外，汎銓已成功自主研發並組裝完成 3 台矽光子分析設備，其功能即是協助
客戶進行光損及漏光定位，並開發從晶片背面進行收光偵測的技術，鎖定矽光
子常用的 1310nm、1550nm 波段，協助定位 PIC 元件、光耦合及封裝過程中
的異常位置，且其核心光損偵測裝置技術已順利取得台灣、日本、美國及韓國
專利。對汎銓更具戰略意義的是，將不再侷限於檢測分析服務供應商的角色，
而是應客戶強烈需求，規劃進一步推出適用於量產端與品質驗證使用的矽光子
測試設備。標準版設備 9 月開放客戶驗證，11/5 正式對外實機展示，今年底開
始銷售，預期今年首批有 2~3 台設備可出貨，明年目標出貨 10 台，標準機台
售價約 6,000 多萬元，此設備毛利率預期在 50%以上水準，若額外選配各項高
階測試模組，單台售價可提升至 1 億至 3 億元。汎銓近年積極建立 AI 晶片分析
平台，並同步發展矽光子檢測、自主設 備開發與專利授權等新成長動能，期望

Document 247: Altman提出AI发展的三条“终局之路”：从国际监管到“一家独霸”

Source PDF: all report pdf/Altman提出AI发展的三条“终局之路”：从国际监管到“一家独霸”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Sam Altman 向 OpenAI 员⼯罕⻅地摊牌了 AI 发展的三条 “ 终局路径 ” ：建⽴ IAEA 式国际监管
机构、合并各⼤ AI 实验室，以及由单⼀公司垄断算⼒ “ 熔毁竞争对⼿ GPU” 。他明确⽀持第
⼀条，并直⾔第三条 “ 显然很糟糕 ” ，同时暗指该⽅案与 Anthropic 存在关联。
摘要：
当 AI 竞赛加速推进， OpenAI 掌⻔⼈罕⻅地向员⼯摊牌了三种可能改变整个⾏业格局的未来。
10 ⽉ 19 ⽇，据 The Information 报道， OpenAI CEO Sam Altman 上周在内部向员⼯描述了业界讨
论中的三条 AI 安全发展路径：建⽴类似国际原⼦能机构的国际监管组织、将各⼤ AI 实验室合并为
单⼀实体，以及由⼀家公司垄断算⼒、 " 熔毁所有竞争对⼿的 GPU" 。此番表态恰逢特朗普政府着
⼿建⽴⾸套针对 " 超级智能 " 开发商的监管机制之际。
Altman 表⽰， OpenAI 在未来⼀年内需要决定究竟推动哪条路径。他同时强调，希望⼈类始终掌

这⼀构想需要所有前沿模型开发国参与国际合作。
第⼆条路径被 Altman 称为 "Demis ⽅案 "—— 援引⾃ Alphabet 旗下 DeepMind 前负责⼈ Demis
Hassabis 约⼗年前提出的构想：建⽴⼀个类似欧洲核⼦研究中⼼（ CERN ）的机构，将主要 AI 公
司有效合并或统⼀输出成果，向合作国家提供新模型。
第三条路径， Altman 称之为 "Eliezer ⽅案 " ，得名于知名 AI 安全理论家 Eliezer Yudkowsky 。其
核⼼逻辑是：当某家 AI 开发商率先实现递归⾃我改进（ RSI ）后，利⽤由此获得的经济实⼒，剥
夺其他所有开发商的算⼒资源， " 熔毁所有竞争对⼿的 GPU" 。
对于第三条路径， Altman 态度明确： " 这显然很糟糕。 " 他表⽰将其纳⼊讨论，仅是为了 " 完整
性 " 。
第三条路 “Eliezer ⽅案 ” 暗指 Anthropic ？
知情⼈⼠透露，由于 Yudkowsky 与 Anthropic 核⼼成员存在渊源， Altman 对 "Eliezer ⽅案 " 的定性

—— 即将先进 AI 集中于单⼀公司控制之下 —— 被部分员⼯解读为对 Anthropic 的隐晦批评。
Anthropic ⽅⾯同样在积极推动⾃⾝的 AI 安全议程。该公司近期向国会议员提交了⼀份法案草
案，建议授权商务部在 AI 可能造成超过 10 亿美元损失或导致 50 ⼈以上死亡或重伤的情况下，叫
停相关实验室的模型训练或使⽤，并要求 AI 实验室每年⾄少接受两次独⽴安全评估。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
与此同时， Meta CEO 扎克伯格和英伟达 CEO ⻩仁勋等⼈则持反对⽴场，认为此类监管将对新兴
开发商造成过重负担，形成 " 监管俘获 " 。
⾏业协调难题与安全压⼒并存
Altman 坦承，前两条路径均需要企业或政府之间⼤规模协调，⽽ OpenAI ⽬前 " ⽆法在竞争中让
步 " 。 OpenAI 与主要竞争对⼿ Anthropic 均在加速推进 IPO 进程。
内部安全压⼒同样不容忽视。据报道，近期多起 AI 安全事件引发⼴泛关注 ——OpenAI 、
Anthropic 、⾕歌和 Meta 的模型均出现⼊侵或破坏其他公司乃⾄⾃⾝系统的情况，即便模型 " 知
道 " ⾃⼰不应采取此类⾏动。 OpenAI ⽬前处于全⼒应对状态，致⼒于在发布下⼀个重⼤模型前建

⽴完善的安全机制。
OpenAI 内部的安全⽂化也受到员⼯质疑。部分员⼯私下对公司的安全承诺表⽰担忧，起因是公
司以 " 不当向外部机构分享信息 " 为由，解雇了三名专注于安全研究的员⼯。
在⾏业层⾯， OpenAI 、⾕歌和 Anthropic 正联合筹建 " 前沿 AI 标准局 " （ Standards Authority for
Frontier AI ），计划于年底或 2027 年初落地。该机构将⽀持嵌⼊各 AI 开发商内部的独⽴评估⼈
员，确保安全实践落地，并规范安全与安全事件的报告机制。不过，各⽅在新机构是否应⾃⾏
开展安全与能⼒测试，还是委托外部机构执⾏等问题上，仍存在分歧。
限免
请发表您的评论

Document 292: CTCP Nhựa Bình Minh - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Nhựa Bình Minh - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Company)
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 tăng trưởng chậm 
nhưng biên lợi nhuận gộp vẫn được 
duy trì do tăng giá bán

Sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 chững lại 
do tâm lý nhập hàng thận trọng và 
hoạt động xây dựng phục hồi chậm

Biên lợi nhuận gộp được dự báo sẽ 
thu hẹp cuối năm, phản ánh chi phí 
nguyên liệu cao hơn trong 1Q2026

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu/LNST 2Q2026 đạt lần lượt 1,320/367 tỷ VND (+1%/+11% YoY). Lũy kế 
6M2026, doanh thu đạt 2,777 tỷ VND (+3% YoY) trong khi LNST ước đạt 671 tỷ VND 
(+9% YoY). Biên lãi gộp 6M2026 cải thiện đạt 47.0% (+240 bps YoY), chủ yếu do việc 
tăng giá bán giúp doanh nghiệp bù đắp được mức tăng của chi phí đầu vào.

Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck. Việc 
BMP thu hẹp các chương trình chiết khấu, với mức chiết khấu bình quân khoảng 
5.2% trong 2Q2026, làm giảm động lực nhập hàng của các đại lý và khiến sản lượng 
tiêu thụ chậm lại. Dù vậy, thị phần toàn quốc của BMP ước đạt 28% (+1 đpt svck) 
nhờ các doanh nghiệp khác trong ngành gặp khó khăn khi giá hạt nhựa tăng mạnh.

Biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP dự kiến thu hẹp trong 2H2026 trước áp lực chi phí 
nguyên liệu đầu vào tăng. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi kỳ vọng mức độ ảnh hưởng sẽ thấp 
hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành do (1) các lô nguyên liệu giá cao từ 
1Q2026 đã được đưa vào sản xuất và tiêu thụ một phần trong 1H2026; (2) BMP chủ 
động nâng gấp đôi hàng tồn kho để ứng phó với biến động bất lợi của giá nguyên 
liệu; (3) hơn 90% nhu cầu nguyên liệu uPVC được cung ứng từ các đối tác trong 
nước như hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam, AGC Chemicals và LSP; và (4) công ty duy 
trì giá bán bình quân ở mức cao.

Dựa trên kết quả kinh doanh và định giá, chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP với cổ 
phiếu BMP, giá mục tiêu là 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu (tương ứng với mức tăng 14.3% 
so với giá đóng cửa ngày 23/09/2026).

Tăng trưởng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất Hoạt động kinh doanh

Nhựa Bình Minh (BMP) là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực sản xuất ống nhựa với thị phần 
dẫn đầu tại thị trường nội địa (27% vào cuối 
2023), thị trường chính tập trung tại khu vực 
miền Nam. BMP có 4 nhà máy với tổng công suất 
đạt 150,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: BMP, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Kỳ vọng đà phục hồi của xây dựng dân dụng và bất động sản miền Nam sẽ thúc đẩy sản lượng tiêu 
thụ tăng trưởng trong 2026–2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Giá PVC được kỳ vọng sẽ hạ nhiệt và khả năng giữ giá bán đầu ra ở mức cao giúp BMP giữ biên lợi 
nhuận gộp ở nền cao. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

### Page 4

4

Nhu cầu nhập hàng thận trọng và 
cắt giảm các đợt chiết khấu trong 
kỳ của BMP làm giảm sản lượng tiêu 
thụ trong 2Q2026

Sự hồi phục của nhu cầu xây dựng 
dân dụng và nguồn cung các dự án 
bất động sản tại miền Nam sẽ thúc 
đẩy tăng trưởng sản lượng 2H2026 
 Sản lượng tiêu thụ trong 2Q2026 ước đạt 20,100 tấn, giảm 11% svck, nâng tổng 
sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck do các yếu tố bất 
lợi từ bên ngoài như (1) giá hạt nhựa tăng cao khiến thị trường dè chừng trong 
việc nhập hàng vì lo ngại nguồn cung bất ổn (2) BMP đã thu hẹp các chương 
trình chiết khấu so với 2025 ngay trước thời điểm xung đột bùng nổ, giúp tỷ lệ 
chi phí bán hàng/doanh thu về mức 11.3%, giảm 800 bps so với 1Q2026 ở mức 
19.3%, nhưng đồng thời cũng khiến sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP suy giảm do 
chính sách bán hàng thắt chặt hơn với các đối thủ có thị phần thấp hơn.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP dần hồi phục từ 3Q2026 khi nhu 
cầu nhập hàng của các đại lý cải thiện sau giai đoạn thận trọng trong nửa đầu 
năm. Về cuối năm, hoạt động xây dựng dự kiến tăng tốc khi điều kiện thi công 
thuận lợi hơn sau mùa mưa. Bên cạnh đó, việc triển khai các dự án nhà ở tại 
TP.HCM và các tỉnh lân cận được kỳ vọng hỗ trợ nhu cầu ống nhựa trong giai 
đoạn tới. Theo đó, chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP lần lượt đạt 
96,695/100,125/96,750 tấn trong 2026/2027/2028.

Biểu đồ 3. Tương quan giữa tỷ lệ chi phí bán hàng/doanh 
thu và lợi nhuận thuần/sản lượng tiêu thụ  
 Biểu đồ 4. Dự phóng nguồn cung bất động sản tại Hà Nội và Hồ 
Chí Minh

Nguồn: BMP, KBSV  Nguồn: CBRE, KBSV

Biểu đồ 5. Vòng quay hàng tồn kho của BMP so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành 
   BMP duy trì vòng quay dẫn đầu trong 
ngành nhờ lợi thế nguồn nguyên liệu và thị 
phần đứng đầu tại miền Nam. Tuy nhiên, 
xu hướng suy giảm các quý gần đây về 
mức 5.3 lần, giảm 0.21 điểm QoQ do nhu 
cầu tiêu thụ toàn ngành suy giảm.

Nguồn: Fiinpro, KBSV

### Page 5

5

Lợi thế từ hệ sinh thái công ty mẹ 
SCG và đối tác Nhật Bản giúp BMP 
có nhiều dư địa để nâng cao hiệu 
quả hoạt động

Theo chia sẻ từ ban lãnh đạo, BMP có lợi thế vì nhập nguyên liệu từ công ty mẹ 
là hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam và đối tác Nhật Bản là AGC Chemicals Vietnam, 
giúp giảm được 5% thuế nhập khẩu và chi phí vận chuyển. Điều này giúp BMP 
hưởng lợi kép khi có thể tăng được giá bán theo thị trường nhưng nguyên vật 
liệu rẻ hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành. KBSV nhận thấy định hướng 
dài hạn của BMP là cân bằng lợi ích cổ đông và khách hàng, vì vậy việc giảm giá 
bán để giành thị phần không phải lựa chọn ưu tiên của doanh nghiệp mà là 
nâng cao tỷ suất lợi nhuận/sản phẩm thành phẩm.

KBSV dự báo biên lợi nhuận gộp thu hẹp trong 2H2026 khi chi phí nguyên liệu 
giá cao từ 1H2026 được phản ánh vào giá vốn, và hồi phục trong 2027–2028 
nhờ đặc tính giữ giá bán ít thay đổi của nhóm nhựa. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá PVC 
resin chịu áp lực giảm từ (1) nhu cầu xây dựng tại Trung Quốc còn yếu khi thị 
trường bất động sản chưa hồi phục rõ ràng; và (2) các nhà sản xuất giải phóng 
hàng tồn kho sau mùa mưa, làm tăng sản lượng hạt nhựa PVC resin. Theo đó, 
KBSV dự phóng biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP lên 46.4%/46.7%/46.5% trong 
2026/2027/2028.

gộp của BMP

### Page 1

13030323     1

Economy 
Kicking and screaming: Child care costs persist 
20 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Child care remains a growing financial burden for many households, even as growth in the median payment has moderated, 
according to Bank of America payments data. More families are paying more than $2,000 per month for care, suggesting 
affordability pressures are becoming increasingly concentrated among households facing the highest expenses. 
• Child care cost pressures vary significantly across the country. Nashville and Jacksonville, FL posted the fastest year-over-year 
growth among major metro areas, while New York and Washington DC saw the biggest decrease in median payment growth, 
according to Bank of America payments data. 
• Limited supply may continue to constrain access to child care for some households. Child care employment fell 0.7% year-over-
year in September, suggesting provider capacity remains under pressure and could continue to affect household finances and 
labor force participation.

Child care costs outpace rent and medical services 
Child care remains a significant expense for many families. In September, the growth in child care and other school-related fees 
surpassed other major household expenses such as rent and medical services, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) 
(Exhibit 1). 
Yet, while child care prices continue to rise in official inflation data, median household payments have largely moderated since 
February, according to Bank of America data (Exhibit 2). More importantly, the growth in the number of households paying for 
child care has fallen since the start of 2025. 
Exhibit 1: The cost of child care has surpassed other services such as 
rent in the consumer price index  
Consumer price index by category (monthly, seasonally adjusted, YoY%)

Source: Haver Analytics  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 2: The median child care payment per household was up 
1.0% year-over-year (YoY) in September but the number of 
households paying for child care fell 1.0% YoY 
Median child care payment per household and number of households 
(monthly, three-month moving average, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Fewer lower-income households are paying for child care 
Why is Bank of America payment growth trending in the opposite direction of the Consumer Price Index? Households are likely 
changing the amount of care they use, which is reflected in slower growth in the number paying for child care. As more

households shift into lower-cost care (school-age, public pre-K, subsidized or free after-school programs), median payments can 
fall even as prices continue rising.  
The decline in the number of households paying for child care may also reflect families seeking alternatives to formal care 
arrangements. Some may be relying more heavily on relatives, shifting parents’ work schedules or dropping out of the workforce 
altogether to care for their children in response to elevated costs. This could explain why, among all income groups paying for 
care, the number of lower-income households has seen the biggest decline, falling 2.0% year-over-year (YoY) in September 
(Exhibit 3). 
Exhibit 3: The number of lower-income households paying for child care has fallen the most, down 2.0% YoY in September 
Number of households with child care payments (monthly, three-month moving average, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
More families face larger monthly bills 
Yet even as fewer households pay for child care, the share of those shelling out more than $2,000 per month has increased 
substantially (Exhibit 4). In fact, it has more than doubled since 2023, reaching 7.7% in 2026 year-to-date. Meanwhile, the share 
of those paying $1,000 or less has declined, suggesting higher monthly payments are becoming more common even as the 
national median grows more slowly. 
Exhibit 4: The share of households paying >$2000 a month for child care has more than doubled since 2023 
Monthly child care payments by amount (annual average, share of households, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Rising costs take a bigger bite out of households’ paychecks 
What makes child care “affordable”? According to the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services, child care is considered 
affordable if it accounts for no more than 7% of family income. Yet, in Bank of America data, the average child care cost was 8% 
in September.  
While an 8% share may not appear dramatically above the federal affordability benchmark of 7%, it represents an average across 
households’ income levels. For families in high-cost metro areas or those paying more than $2,000 per month, the burden is 
likely considerably higher.

Notably, lower- and middle-income households see outsized shares of their pay dedicated to child care in Bank of America 
payments data, though this has moderated slightly from 2025 (Exhibit 5).  
Exhibit 5: In September, the average child care payment was 8.6% of lower- and middle-income total households’ pay, whereas for higher-income 
families it accounted for 7.3% 
Average child care payment as a share of pay in September by income (annual, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Where child care costs are climbing 
Child care pressures also differ substantially by location. In Bank of America payments data, Nashville posted the fastest YoY 
median payment growth in September, followed by Jacksonville, FL and Phoenix (Exhibit 6). This dispersion of growth across the 
country suggests child care cost pressures are no longer confined to historically expensive coastal markets.  
Interestingly, New York and Washington DC saw the sharpest decline in growth this year. Why might this be? The supply side of 
the market may help explain why – for example, New York City recently introduced universal Pre-K.1  
Exhibit 6: Nashville and Jacksonville, FL saw the greatest increase in median child care payment growth among select cities in September 
Median child care payments by core-based statistical area in September (YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Fewer providers, higher costs 
As of September 2026, employment in child care services was down 0.7% from a year earlier, suggesting the sector’s workforce 
recovery has stalled. Fewer workers can constrain the number of available places or the hours providers offer. That may leave 
families facing longer searches, fewer workable arrangements or continued price pressure. In turn, access to care can influence 
parents’ ability to enter, remain in or expand their participation in the workforce. 
Exhibit 7: Employment within child care services fell 0.7% YoY in September 
Employees by sector (monthly, seasonally adjusted, YoY%)

Source: Haver Analytics  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
What are the economic implications of rising child care costs? 
Child care costs may have important labor market implications for families with young children. Of those households paying for 
child care in 2026, more than half of them were Older Millennials (52%), followed by Younger Millennials (20%) and Gen X (16%), 
according to Bank of America payments data. This means child care costs weigh more heavily on Older Millennials. 
The share of Older Millennial households receiving more than one paycheck has continued to grow from a year earlier, but the 
pace of that improvement has moderated over the past three months (Exhibit 8). Given that this group accounts for the majority 
of households paying for child care in Bank of America payments data, they may offer early signs of how affordability affects 
their employment decisions. 
Exhibit 8: The share of Older Millennial households paying for child care that have more than one person with a paycheck has declined since 2021  
Share of households with a child care payment with more than one person with a paycheck (%, monthly, three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Child care challenges can weigh on women’s employment 
According to data from the American Time Use Survey, women continue to perform significantly more child care and household 
work than men, even as their participation in paid work has increased. This suggests that persistent gaps in access and 
affordability may have disproportionate consequences for women’s employment. Indeed, the percentage of women who cited 
child care as a reason for temporary unemployment was nearly three times higher than men’s in 2026, though the average fell 
from 2025, according to data from the BLS. 
Yet labor force participation data indicates a more nuanced picture. Despite persistent child care pressures, mothers are 
participating in the workforce at rates that remain above their 2019 averages.

The strongest gains have occurred among women with children under age six, whose labor force participation rate remains well

by a smaller margin.  
Exhibit 9: In 2025, women with children under age six saw the greatest increase in their participation rate compared to the 2019 average 
Annual estimates for labor force participation rates by sex and age of children (annual averages, indexed, 2019 average = 100)

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Mothers’ workforce participation remains elevated, but recent gains have slowed  
However, recent momentum has slowed. While mothers of young children experienced particularly large participation gains in 
2022 and 2023, those have partially reversed in 2024 and 2025 (Exhibit 10).  
In contrast, participation among women with children ages 6 to 17 has continued to edge higher. This signals that the post-
pandemic increase in working has largely been sustained in this group, but the pace of improvement has moderated, especially 
for women caring for younger children. 
Exhibit 10: The labor force participation rate for women with children under age six fell in 2025 from the prior year 
Annual estimates for labor force participation rates by sex and age of children (annual averages, percentage point difference from prior year)

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Bank of America credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card holders 
making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards is excluded. Data regarding 
merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial services 
companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), 
which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
If applicable, the consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level.  
If applicable, any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a 
deposit account or credit card. Any reference to aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-
peer, cash and checks.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 363: Oil prices hold firm despite Persian Gulf supply growing

Source PDF: all report pdf/Oil prices hold firm despite Persian Gulf supply growing.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5

### Page 1

Oil prices remain ﬁrm despite rising supply from the Persian
Gulf. We expect oil prices to trade lower into year-end. More
positive signs of a potential US-Iran deal would give us more
conﬁdence in this view. Meanwhile, European gas looks
vulnerable heading deeper into the heating season
Warren Patterson
Oil prices are holding ﬁrm despite Persian Gulf supply increasing
Persian Gulf oil ﬂows pick-up
There are few signs of de-escalation in the Middle East. If anything,

it appears as though the conﬂict is broadening, with the Houthis in
Yemen increasingly targeting Saudi Arabia. Despite little
improvement in the geopolitical environment, oil ﬂows from the
Persian Gulf have been trending higher with supply from the region
returning to 70-75% of pre-war levels, although there are some
estimates suggesting we could be back to as high as 80-90% of
pre-war ﬂows.

### Page 2

The fact that oil prices are well-supported despite the improvement
in ﬂows from the region appears to reﬂect concerns over how
sustainable this supply is, particularly when considering the
geopolitical backdrop. The market continues to price in a large risk
premium in the absence of a deal between the US and Iran. And to
see this risk premium erode, the market may need the comfort of a
deal. Failing that, time may be enough for oil prices to trend lower,

assuming supplies from the region continue to hold up. For now, we
are holding onto our 4Q26 Brent forecast of $80/bbl and FY27
forecast of $71/bbl. However, given the high level of uncertainty,
this is a low-conviction call, speciﬁcally for 4Q26.
As oil ﬂows from the Persian Gulf have picked up, another
bottleneck has become increasingly noticeable in the oil market.
There is signiﬁcant tightness in the tanker market, which has seen
tanker freight rates surge to record levels. Oil ﬂows from the Persian

Gulf have become increasingly ineﬃcient due to the challenging
environment, which has absorbed more of the global tanker ﬂeet.
And the only way this starts to normalise is if the way oil ﬂows
from the region also starts to normalise.
The other pain point for the oil market remains diesel, which will be
a concern as we head further into the Northern Hemisphere winter.
Disruptions from the Persian Gulf and Russia have tightened the
market, while the risk of export restrictions from the US has kept the

market nervous. The risk of US export restrictions appears to have
subsided, with Europe set to release diesel from strategic reserves.
Furthermore, expensive freight suggests that reﬁned product prices
are likely to remain elevated.
Natural gas vulnerable heading into heating
season
European natural gas prices have traded at their highest level since
December 2022, with supply concerns growing as the region heads
into the heating season. Unfortunately for the natural gas market,

we have not seen a similar recovery in LNG ﬂows from the Persian
Gulf as we have with oil. Therefore, LNG ﬂows from the region
remain down about 80% year-on-year. The market is increasingly

This publication has been prepared by ING solely for information purposes
irrespective of a particular user's means, ﬁnancial situation or investment
accepting that Persian Gulf LNG could remain largely absent from
global markets for much of the Northern Hemisphere winter. This
means Asia and Europe will continue competing for spot cargoes
throughout the winter, keeping prices well supported and leaving
the market vulnerable to further spikes.
European gas storage remains a concern, at just under 75% full,

well below the ﬁve-year average of 89%. Low storage has seen the
German government instruct state-owned SEFE to buy and store an
additional 8TWh of natural gas by 15 December. The prospect of
essentially forced buying provides further support to the market.
With little sign of an imminent recovery in Persian Gulf LNG supply,
weather will be crucial this winter, and there is growing expectation
that Europe will experience a winter of milder temperatures due to

El Niño, which would provide some relief to the market. However, it
is not a strong relationship, and El Niño does not guarantee milder
temperatures. The North Asia winter will also be important for the
LNG market, as El Niño can also typically mean milder
temperatures in this region.
We now expect TTF to average EUR73/MWh over the 26/27 winter,
up from a previous forecast of EUR65/MWh. The market will likely be
very volatile over this period, given the lower starting point for gas

storage. Prices are then expected to sell oﬀ aggressively from 2Q27
as Persian Gulf supply normalises, and we see a further ramp-up in
US LNG export capacity.

Document 378: Sales Trader Commentary_ TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally_ _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sales Trader Commentary_ TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally_ _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

Sales Trader Commentary: TAIEX 49k Wobble -
July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa
Rally? | GSTW
13 October 2026 | 4:11 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Index lost the 49k points threshold and saw the biggest decline on a point basis since
the end of July. Market seemed to lose the steam to break through 50k points after
the past choppy sessions. Is this July de ja vu or some healthy pullback? Foreigners

and domestic investors seem to share different narrative. Check out below for our
investor feedback.
Key Investor Feedback and Observations
Positioning. we saw meaningful outflows today from all kinds of investors today,
again focusing in ex-TSMC large caps. Today foreigners net sold US$5.2bn today,
the second largest in the history (simultaneously drove TWD weakness over 32.23
today). Domestic investors, especially retail, on the other hand, anchored the

market with the record high margin loan balance (as of 10/12/2026). Local funds
remained buyer as well mainly thanks to ETFs inflow.
Macro. CPI headline brought uncertainty and forced global investors to turn
cautious, which was exacerbated by high yields and crude. For Taiwan, AI trade is
relatively resilient to the Middle Eastern tension and high energy prices. However,
at some point, it is going to have a real economic impact either through Fed or

some other macro data.
Sentiment. Surprisingly, people are still bullish in Taiwan, though they also
acknowledge some choppy trading sessions ahead. Local investor feedback is not
calling for capitulation; in fact, most believe this is just another "two-steps forward
one-step backward" cadence towards another record high. Nonetheless, some
retail start taking chips off the table. This echoes our observation among the
foreign investors. Yes, the AI fundamental future is all sunshine, but as we head into

the end of year, it is natural for them to protect their great fun thus far this year
instead of adding on immediate risk. I'd imagine the next few weeks are going to be
full of 1) rapid sector rotation; 2) weak or unsustainable momentum before Santa
comes into town after the midterm election for our year-end rally Christmas gift.
TSMC Earnings and Others. We know the numbers are going to be good, and we
also know people are going to sell-on-facts after TSMC earnings call; recall most

recent one happened right after the meeting that scarred a lot of investors since.
Perhaps this time people started trimming ahead (and maybe too early?), which
provides some window for a quick rebound into small and mid cap. OCP summit is
happening right now, and TPCA Show will start next week. Watch out for single
stock movement mentioned in the conferences.
10/13/26, 4:13 PM Sales Trader Commentary: TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally? | GSTW

https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/812c043a-8535-4003-887e-b01f79096974 1/8

### Page 2

Sector. 1) ABF/CCL is still the crowded long sectors for both foreign and domestic
investor, but echoing on above points, investors need more catalysts to add on
risks or drive the re-ratings. 2) Power is another big issues that data centers need
to resolve, power solutions and fuel cells name are also key focus in Taiwan. 3) Let's
not forget about OSATs. We need more equipment and testing capacity to suffice
enlarging demand from both ASIC and GPU.

Key Stocks I Follow: Unimicron, Kinsus, NYPCB, ZDT, EMC, TUC, Delta, Lite-On,
Kaori, ASE, KYEC, All Ring
Risk. Upside looks thin in the short term. Good earnings are not enough - look at
Nanya Tech and Memory sector today. Chasing momentum now may get hurt. I also
smelled some short unwind today (i.e. funding short names outperformance such
as Realtek, Novatek, Shipper, etc) - signs of de-leveraging. Directionless is how I
would describe the past few days, but maybe it's time to find some value Buy?

Islamic finance is growing at a rapid pace, driven 
primarily by increasing demand for investors who 
seek Shariah -compliant investments. Sukuk, often 
referred to as Islamic bonds, surpassed USD 1trn in 
2025, making them one of the fastest -growing 
segments o f Islamic finance. The asset class’s 
unique value proposition is driving its popularity, 
especially in an increasingly volatile global  
economic landscape. 
In this publication, we will explore the evolving role of Sukuk in wealth management, 
examining market fundamentals, risk frameworks and emerging opportunities within 
this market.

### Page 3

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  3

Sukuk, commonly referred to as Islamic bonds, are Shariah-compliant investment 
certificates representing ownership interests in underlying assets, usufructs (the 
right to use an asset and receive its benefits, without owning it) or ventures. In 
return, Sukuk holders receive periodic distributions, typically in the form of rental 
payments (Ijarah), profit-sharing (Mudarabah) or asset-generated cash flows, and 
upon maturity the principal amount is repaid. Sukuk differ from conventional 
bonds in several ways: 
Fig. 1  
Sukuk vs. conventional bonds

Aspect  Conventional bonds Sukuk

Core principle  Lending (time-value of money) Asset ownership 
Underlying asset  Any asset, business or project Must be Sharia-compliant 
Returns  Guaranteed (fixed or floating interest) Not legally guaranteed (depends on 
underlying asset’s performance) 
Risk   Issuer bears all performance risk Shared risk between parties 
Transparency  Moderate (depends on disclosure) High (asset identification required)

Source: Standard Chartered

The role of Sukuk in the investing landscape is evolving amid 
global uncertainty and rising geopolitical tensions. Investors 
are increasingly seeking more stable and asset ‑backed 
alternatives. An investor can consider investing in Sukuk for 
the following reasons: 
Resilient 
Sukuk provide asset -backed collateral that conventional 
bonds often lack, offering a cushion during volatile market 
conditions. Historically, Islamic investments, including Sukuk’, 
have been countercyclical, i.e. they tend to expand or remain 
stable when c onventional markets contract. Across major 
market stress from the 2018 US-China Trade War through the 
2026 Middle East Conflict, Sukuk bonds consistently 
outperformed Global Bonds — delivering smaller drawdowns 
in risk-off episodes such as the 2018 US-China trade war and 
2022 higher for longer inflation, and greater resilience in 
geopolitical oil shocks, underscoring the asset class's 
structural defensive characteristics.

Strong fundamentals
The Sukuk market experienced an unprecedented 25% growth in issuance in 2025, with issuance nearly USD 300bn. According
to some  reports,  the  Sukuk  market  could  reach  USD  3trn  by  2030  driven  by  increased  demand  for  Shariah-compliant
investments, sovereign funds’ diversification needs, development of regulatory frameworks for Sukuk and growth in Middle East

capital markets.  Nearly  80%  of  Fitch ‑rated Sukuk  carry  investment ‑grade ratings  and  have  little  or  no  history  of  default,
underscoring the sector’s strong fundamentals. These factors should help to instil confidence among investors, who can look at
Sukuk as central building blocks of their portfolio.

Convergence with sustainable investing trends
Sukuk and sustainable bonds share more common ground than their different origins suggest, making sustainable Sukuk a
natural bridge between Islamic finance and sustainable investing. This also helps explain Sukuk’s growing appeal beyond
traditional Muslim investors, as evidenced by Sustainable Sukuk issuance rising from USD 464mn in 2017 to USD 9bn in 2024,

representing a CAGR of 52.7%. The overlap starts with what Sukuk exclude: Shariah principles prohibit interest, excessive
uncertainty and speculation, and screen out sectors financing harmful industries such as tobacco, gambling, weapons, adult
entertainment and alcohol – a negative screen that closely aligns with the exclusionary (negative screening) method, commonly
used in sustainable investing. Tradi tional Sukuk bonds require proceeds to be linked to identifiable real assets or projects,

creating a ring-fenced structure like use-of-proceeds sustainable bonds. Combined with Shariah’s broader objectives of social
welfare, resource stewardship and equita ble risk-sharing, this creates strong alignment with the United Nations Sustainable
Development Goals.
Green Sukuk is an established example, where proceeds are earmarked for environmental projects such as renewable energy,
clean transport, energy-efficient buildings or sustainable water and wastewater management. Issuers must meet both Shariah

approval from a recognised scholarly board and alignment with International Capital Market Association (ICMA) Green Bond
Principles.
Traditional Sukuk already comply with Shariah laws such as avoiding Riba (interest -based returns arising from lending
transactions), Gharar (investments with ambiguity) and Maysir (speculation). Sustainable Sukuk build on this foundation by
adding environmental and social objectives. The table below highlights how sustainable Sukuk differ from traditional Sukuk.

Fig. 4
Traditional Sukuk vs. Sustainable bonds vs Sustainable Sukuk
Aspect Traditional Sukuk Sustainable Bonds Sustainable Sukuk
Underlying structure Asset-linked
Use of proceeds For any Shariah-compliant
purpose

Digital integration
The integration  of  blockchain  technology  and  digital  platforms  is  revolutionising  Sukuk  issuance  and  trading,  dramatically
reducing transaction costs and expanding accessibility to  a broader  investor base.  For instance, SAKKEX (an  independent
blockchain-based Sukuk marketplace) streamlines drafting, issuance, and near ‑instant settlement. Its Sukuk Wallet App also
allows investors to trade on the go. Several such digital platforms now allow tokenised Sukuk that enable fractional ownership,

automated Shariah  compliance  verification  and  real-time  settlement,  thereby  addressing  challenges  that  have  persisted  in
Islamic finance markets. Digital tools also simplify cross border Sukuk transactions by enabling end to end digital origination and
trading, with smart contracts automating profit sharing to cut operational costs and enhance transparency.

### Page 6

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026 6
Sukuk structures define the nature of ownership, risk -sharing and return
generation. Several types of Sukuk structures exist and are defined by the
underlying Shariah contract. We discuss some prominent ones below and see
how they align with various phases of an investor’s wealth journey:
Sukuk al-Ijarah is a Shariah-compliant lease-based bond where the investor gains partial ownership in a physical

asset such as a building, airport or equipment. The asset is then leased back to the issuer who pays a nominal
(pre-agreed) rent to the investor. An investor seeking stable, predictable income streams with lower volatility can
consider investing in these Sukuk. The original principal amount is returned at the end of the lease period.
Ijarah Sukuk are ideal for the wealth preservation phase of an investor’s wealth journey. For instance, investors
nearing retirement typically need to restructure their portfolios by shifting from growth to preservation while

generating steady income to cover living expenses. Ijarah Sukuk deliver on both fronts: protecting capital through
asset-backed structures while providing predictable rental income that supports household needs without
depleting principal.
Sukuk al-Murabahah are cost-plus financing structures where the issuer buys an asset and immediately resells
it to the investors at a marked-up price, with payment deferred over a pre-determined time. Consider this: Let us

assume, a company needs USD 100m. A special purpose vehicle (SPV), established by the company, mobilises
funds from investors and buys commodities worth USD 100m and immediately resells them to the company for
USD 112m payable over time. The USD 12m difference is the agreed profit margin. Murabahah Sukuk ar e best
suited for investors seeking income over a short to medium term, typically, 1-5 years. Investments in this type of
Sukuk return the principal gradually throughout the investment period rather than only at maturity.

Investments in Murabahah Sukuk are best suited for wealth protection and income generation. Unlike Ijarah which
return principal as a lump sum at maturity, Murabahah amortise both profit and principal in each instalment. This
makes them ideal for retirees who need to systematically draw down capital to fund living expenses without
depleting their portfolio too quickly.
Sukuk al-Mudarabah is a profit-sharing partnership arrangement where investors provide 100% of the capital

and operate as silent partners. The issuer manages the business or project as the active partner. Under a
Mudarabah arrangement, financial losses arising from normal bu siness risks are borne by the investors in
proportion to their capital contribution, while the Mudarib loses its effort and entitlement to profit. However, where
losses result from negligence, misconduct, breach of agreed terms, or misconduct by the Mudari b, the Mudarib
may be held liable. Think of it as venture capital meets bonds; you fund the business, professionals run it and

you share the profits or bear the losses resulting from the business activities. Mudarabah Sukuk are often
regarded as one of the  structures most closely aligned with the Shariah principle of profit and loss sharing.
However, they bear higher risk and lower predictability compared to other structures.
Investors with a growth mindset and open to high -risk tolerance can consider investing in these Sukuk . They
best align with accumulation and growth phase of the wealth journey, typically, when investors have long time

horizons to absorb volatility and can afford capital risk for higher returns. However, to balance risk, it is important
for investors to diversify across various types of Sukuk. Even within Mudarabah, they can consider investing
in multiple projects, sectors and geographies.
1 Sukuk structures and
wealth mandates

### Page 7

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  7

A joint venture partnership structure where both investors and the issuer contribute capital is Sukuk al -
Musharakah. In this structure, both parties (issuer and investors) jointly provide capital, participate in managing 
the venture, and share profits or losses based on agreed upon ratios. This structure appeals to investors focused 
on growth, interested in active participation, and prepared to take on greater risk in pursuit of higher returns. 
Musharakah Sukuk are best suited for the growth and accumulation phase where investors are building wealth 
aggressively, comfortable with high risk and able to lock up capital for the long term (typically 5+ years).

Sukuk al-Wakalah is an agency arrangement where investors appoint a representative from the issuing company 
as their Wakil (agent) to invest their funds in specific Shariah-compliant assets or projects in exchange for a fee. 
This arrangement is similar to a mutual fund or ETF, where the agent manages a diversified pool of Shariah 
compliant assets on behalf of the investors. Investors benefit from professional fund management, diversification, 
transparency and flexibility. Depending on issuer preferences, Wakalah Sukuk can b e structured with lower 
investment thresholds, making them accessible to a wide range of investors. 
Wakalah Sukuk suitability depends heavily on the underlying asset allocation and risk profile. Conservative 
Wakalah structures investing in stable, income -generating assets suit wealth preservation. Moderate -risk 
Wakalah can complement accumulation portfolios as a diversification tool, though not as a primary growth driver 
as their structure and risk mandate limit upside potential.

Sukuk al-Istisna is a project financing structure based on a manufacturing or construction contract. Investors 
provide upfront capital to finance the construction of specific assets. Upon completion, the asset is sold to an end 
buyer (often the original issuer or a third party) who makes deferred payments including the cost plus a mark-up, 
which are then distributed to investors. Istisna suit seasoned investors with construction finance expertise and 
those who can absorb development risk and commit capital for the long term. Owing to its complexity and higher 
risk profile, this type of Sukuk typically requires large initial investments, making it less accessible to retail 
investors.  
In the wealth cycle, Istisna are best aligned with the accumulation and growth phase, when long time horizons 
can absorb construction delays and development risks. The long-term commitment and potential for cost overruns 
or project failure make it unsuitable for later wealth stages such as retirement.

Sukuk al -Salam is an Islamic bond backed by a forward purchase contract where investors pay upfront for 
commodities to be delivered later. At its core, they are similar to forward contracts. However, unlike a forward 
contract, Salam contracts are fully prepaid, standardi sed and designed with clearly defined delivery terms to 
reduce uncertainty (gharar). Under this arrangement, an investor profits from the difference between the lower 
Salam purchase price (paid today) and the higher resale price (received at delivery).  
Salam Sukuk carry high delivery and price risk and aligns well with investors in the growth and accumulation 
phase. Salam requires strong commodity‑market expertise, deep understanding of production and delivery risks, 
and the ability to assess the issuer’s operational capacity to deliver the specified goods on time.

Owing to their ethical framework and asset -backed nature, Sukuk are often
considered relatively less risky than conventional bonds. However, they are not
immune to risks. Unique challenges such as asset quality deterioration and
structure-specific exposures require rigorous risk management.

Due diligence in Sukuk extends beyond traditional credit
analysis and an investor should also consider the following:
• Shariah compliance verification can be done by evaluating

the Sukuk structure and confirming that its mandates and
underlying assets fully align with Islamic principles.
• Evaluating asset quality by verifying tangibility, cash -
generating capability and legal ownership can help
investor reduce risk.
• Assessing creditworthiness and market depth will help in
identifying financially strong Sukuk issuers and ensuring
adequate liquidity.
• Cross-border Sukuk investments remain viable,
contingent on careful scrutiny of legal documentation

across multiple jurisdictions to ensure enforceability and
investor protection.
Robust due diligence and proactive risk mitigation strategies
are essential to protect capital and stay aligned with Islamic
financial principles.
1 Risk management and due diligence

### Page 10

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  10

Staying up to date on the latest developments in 
the Sukuk market is important as this space is 
constantly evolving and rapidly expanding. 
Moreover, Islamic finance is not static; Shariah 
interpretations related to Sukuk structures are 
periodically redefined. Staying informed protects 
investors from investing in non -compliant 
options. We look at some of the factors that are 
guiding the future of this landscape:

1 Emerging trends

Green Sukuk issuance is projected to grow materially through 
2030, reflecting the natural convergence of Islamic finance and 
sustainability principles. Key sectors include renewable 
energy, green buildings, low -carbon transportation, water and 
waste management and sustainable agriculture.

Tech advancements  are revolutionising Sukuk origination, 
trading and accessibility. Digitisation offers numerous benefits 
to the Islamic finance landscape: streamlined issuance and 
trading processes, enhanced accessibility through 
tokenisation, immutable audit trails via bl ockchain technology 
and improved secondary market liquidity.

A major regulatory shift is likely to create standardised rules 
across Islamic finance markets globally. The pivotal 
development is AAOIFI's Standard 62, launching in 2026, 
which mandates transparent, Shariah-compliant asset-backing 
and genuine asset ownership. This can fundament ally 
strengthen the Islamic authenticity of Sukuk structures.

The Sukuk market is rapidly expanding beyond its traditional 
Islamic finance strongholds. Non-traditional territories  in 
Africa (e.g., Algeria, Benin and Nigeria), Europe (e.g., 
Luxemburg, UK) and Asia (e.g., Turkey, Pakistan) are 
emerging as Islamic capital market gateways. This expansion 
underscores the evolution of Sukuk from a niche Islamic 
product to a globally reco gnised asset class attracting diverse 
sovereigns and investors across continents.

### Page 11

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  11

Conclusion

Ultimately, Sukuk demonstrate that Shariah compliance and sound investment 
strategy are not competing priorities, but complementary ones. By ensuring that 
portfolios are grounded in real economic activity by offering stability through 
tangible asset -backing and transparent structures that channel capital towards 
productive use. Their foundation in ethical, responsible financing appeals to 
investors seeking both resilience and purpose. As global markets increasingly 
prioritise integrity and long-term sustainability, Sukuk stand out as a compelling tool 
for Shariah -compliant investors who want meaningful diversification without 
compromising on their religious and ethical principles.

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 3.0% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.5% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averag ing 3.2% in 2026 and 3.4% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. 
The BoE is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

歐洲市場問答：高利環境下的壓力與機會
1
Thematic Report
重點提示
中東地緣衝突自2026年2月底爆發以來仍陷僵局，能源價格維
持高檔的時間較預期更長，令歐元區通膨壓力與升息預期升溫。
另一方面，歐洲政府擴大財政支出，公債供給增加，加上歐洲
央行持續縮表，推升期限溢價，令歐洲公債殖利率大幅走升，
德國十年期公債殖利率9/28升破3.6%，創2009年6月以來新高。
本報告透過問答，分析歐洲公債殖利率走高的原因、主權債務
與財政風險、政府因應措施、高利率對企業的影響，以及高利
率環境下的歐洲股債展望。
◼ 本波歐洲公債殖利率走高的驅動因素：能源價格上漲推升通
膨、市場重估歐央貨幣政策路徑，同時，財政支出令歐洲公

債供給增加與歐央持續縮表使私人部門需要吸收更多公債供
給，推升期限溢價，財政疑慮使部分歐洲國家利差承受額外
壓力，加上全球利率上升的外溢影響，共同推升殖利率走高。
◼ 歐洲政府調整發債結構，央行備有穩市工具：歐洲政府縮短
發債期限以控制融資成本，預估此趨勢將延續至2027年；
萬一主權債市場出現無序波動，歐洲央行有OMT與TPI政策
工具，但不會輕易動用。
◼ 整體歐洲債務風險尚可控但中長期壓力持續累積：目前利率
水準尚未對整體債務可持續性構成普遍威脅，但財政脆弱性
較高的國家壓力升高，法國尤其需要關注。財政與政治變數
使法國資產風險溢價維持高位，目前股債定價已反映相當程
度的負面風險。
◼ 德國公債殖利率短期將維持高檔：歐洲央行12月仍有再升息
可能，待能源價格與通膨壓力緩和、升息預期降溫後，利率
才有回落空間，但難降至3%以下。
◼ 歐洲企業資產負債表健康、獲利具韌性，緩衝高利率衝擊。
◼ 高利率限制歐股估值擴張，歐股維持中立，聚焦獲利成長與
政策支撐的產業：銀行受惠於目前利率環境與信貸需求，工

業、電氣化/能源安全及AI受惠戰略自主與資本支出提升。
Sep. 29, 2026
林亞瑱
歐洲總經政策與市場分析

德國公債殖利率曲線上移，短率升幅大於長率
中東衝突發生以來德國公債殖利率曲線(中東地緣衝突前 vs. 目前)
近期全球公債殖利率大幅上揚 ，歐洲國家亦然，德國10年期公
債殖利率9/28升破3.6%，創2009年6月來新高，若從2月底中

Q1：什麼因素推動今年歐洲公債殖利率走高？
(1)能源價格上漲推升通膨、市場重估歐央貨幣政策路徑，為驅
動本波利率上揚的主因：
2月底中東衝突爆發以來 ，德國10年期公債殖利率與油氣價格
走勢高度相關。此外，德國公債各期限殖利率全面上移 ，其中
短率升幅高於長率 ，反映本波利率上揚主 要受到能源價格上升
所帶動的通膨風險，以及市場對ECB政策路徑重新定價的影響。
此外，美國殖利率上升透過全球資本市場產生外溢效果 ，帶動
全球無風險利率中樞上移 ，亦對歐洲公債殖利率形成上行壓力 。

■淨債券發行 ■ECB縮表 ◆對私人部門的淨供給
(2)歐洲公債供給增加、央行持續縮表，推升期限溢價：
德國擴大財政支出，歐洲政府融資需求及公債供給增加；同時 ，
ECB持續進行資產負債表正常化 ，逐步減少公債持有量 ，使新
增債券供給需由私人投資人吸收 ，推升投資人要求的期限溢價 。
除此之外，政府債務持有者結構轉變 ，個人與海外投資人等對
價格較為敏感的投資人占比增加，使債市更容易受到市場情緒
與金融條件變化的影響。
(3)財政疑慮使部分歐洲國家利差承受額外壓力：
歐洲部分國家面臨較高的政府債務壓力，加上政治局勢限制財
政調整空間，市場對債務可持續性的疑慮難消 。尤其在年度預
算協商期間，財政脆弱性較高的國家如法國 、英國，主權債務

風險重新定價容易反映在長端殖利率及與德債的利差擴大。

涉及特定投資標的之建議。
Q2：面對公債殖利率上升，歐洲政府如何因應？
面對公債殖利率上升 ，歐洲政府調整發債結構 、增加短年期債
券發行，以控制高利率環境下的融資成本 ，預期此趨勢將延續
至2027年；惟縮短債務期限雖可降低當期融資成本，也將提高
中期再融資風險。
Q3：萬一歐洲主權債市場出現無序波動，歐洲有哪些
政策工具可以運用？
歐洲央行主要政策工具有二 ，分別為直接貨幣交易 (OMT)與傳
導保護工具(TPI)。
(1)直接貨幣交易(OMT)：2012年9月推出，源自歐債危機，可
在次級市場購買符合條件國家的1至3年期政府公債，且購買
規模事前不設上限 ，惟須搭配歐洲穩定機制 (ESM)的金融援
助計畫，並具嚴格的附帶條件，使用門檻較高，由主權國家

主動申請。
(2)傳導保護工具 (TPI)：2022年7月推出 ，可在次級市場購買
符合條件國家的 1至10年期政府部門債券 ，目的在於因應與
該國基本面不相稱的無序市場波動 ，避免金融市場分裂影響
貨幣政策傳導。啟動時將評估該國是否符合歐盟財政規範 、
債務是否具備可持續性等條件 ，由歐央評估後決定是否啟動。
不過，OMT與TPI均屬市場失序時的最後手段 ，並非用於壓低
正常的主權債殖利率或消除國家間的基本面利差 ，啟動有嚴格
條件，歐央不會輕易動用，目前此兩項工具均未實際動用過。
高利率下，歐洲政府調整發債、央行備有穩市工具

Q4：歐洲政府債務的可持續性是否有立即風險？
主要歐洲國家除德國與西班牙外 ，IMF預估2026年政府債務佔
GDP比重均超過 100%。同時，近年地緣衝突頻率升高 ，戰略
自主與強化地區安全已成為歐洲核心目標 。為了讓成員國有擴
大國防支出的空間 ，歐盟允許成員國啟動 國家豁免條款，得以
在增加國防支出的同時 ，暫時不受歐盟財政規則約束 ，截至目
前已有18個歐盟會員國申請。國防支出提升，若未搭配增加財
政收入或其他支出調整，將使政府債務維持在較高路徑。
所幸，從利率與經濟成長率差距來看 ，目前利率水準尚未對歐
洲整體債務可持續性構成 立即威脅，但部分高負債國家的債務
壓力仍須留意。過去十年低利率環境的效益逐步消退 ，利率與

經濟成長率的差距 (r－g)較過去不利 ，加上期限溢價回升 ，使
高負債國家的債務動態面臨風險。
檢視歐元區四大經濟體 ，德國、西班牙政府債務的利率低於經
濟成長率，債務動態相對穩健；義大利政府債務 佔GDP比重最
高，所幸在政府控制支出下，自2024年以來維持初級財政盈餘，
有助控制債務比率進一步擴大。法國利率雖略低於經濟成長率 ，
但政府債務已接近 GDP的120%，在財政赤字偏高 、利息支出
持續增加且財政整頓進展有限 的情況下，未來兩年債務佔GDP
比率預期將持續上升，財政脆弱性相對較高。
整體歐洲債務風險可控但中長期壓力正在累積

Q5：近期法德利差顯著擴大，驅動因素與對法國股債
市的影響？
財政與政治不確定性升溫 ，推升法國主權風險溢價 ：法國步入
財政與政治不確定性升溫時期 ，不利的財政前景 ，加上4Q26
預算協商與2027年將舉行總統大選，政治不確定性升溫推升十
年期法德利差9月下旬突破100個基點，9/28升至112個基點、
創2012年6月以來新高。法國總統大選將於2027年4~5月舉行，
4/18舉行第一輪投票 ，若無候選人過半 ，5/2將舉行第二輪投
票。由於法國政治版圖高度分裂 、且極右派支持度領先 ，相較
於過往選舉周期，2027年法國選舉的不確定更高，預估將令市
場在2027年總統大選前維持謹慎情緒。
民調反映政治版圖分裂 ，增加財政政策的不確定性： 民調顯示，
極右派國民聯盟候選人瑪琳·勒龐在總統大選首輪的支持度領先，
超過三成，極左派法國不屈黨候選人梅朗雄 、中間偏右候選人
愛德華·菲利普民調落在15~18%左右。法國大選高機率將會進
入第二輪投票，取決於進入第二輪的候選人 ，目前民調顯示以

中間偏右候選人菲利普最有希望擊敗勒龐 ，若為極左派梅朗雄
進入第二輪，則勒龐勝選機率較高。

股債市影響：財政與政治變數將令法國資產風險溢價維持高位
(1)債市： 巴克萊資本 (2026/8)預估三種情境： (a)正向情境：
中東衝突告一段落 、秋季預算協商順利 、中間派候選人民調上
升，十年期法德利差有機會收斂至60~65個基點；(b)不利情境：
秋季預算協商引發市場雜音 、中東衝突進一步推高升息預期 ，
總統大選民調維持較高不確定性 ，法 德 利差 預 估 將 擴 大 至

95~100個基點；(c)極端情境：沒有中間派候選人進入第二輪
總統大選，法德利差可能突破100個基點。
9/28十年期法德利差已升至 112個基點，顯示市場已反映相當
程度的政治風險，再大幅惡化的空間應有限 。儘管如此，不確
定性短期難消，預估法德利差仍將維持在(b)情境高檔盤整。
(2)股市：法國股市估值相較歐股處於折價，且投資人對法國股
市的配置已明顯降低 ，有助緩和股市大幅修正的風險 。然而，
短線亦缺乏驅動股市行情的催化劑 ，法國經濟成長預測持續下
修、經濟動能落後歐元區 ，加上政治不確定性將影響投資人配
置意願，預估法國股市在2027年4月總統大選前波動仍大 ，金
融與內需導向型企業估值預估將承壓。

Q6：德債利率後續走勢？
貨幣政策正常化與結構因素 ，德債利率中樞上移： 低實質利率
環境已改變，根據歐洲央行執委Schnabel(2025/2)分析，過去
壓低實質利率的部分結構性因素正 在逆轉。一方面，地緣政治
碎片化、氣候變遷及勞動力短缺 ，使中長期通膨上行風險增加；
另一方面，全球由「儲蓄過剩」逐步轉向「債券供給過剩 」，
政府財政赤字擴大與央行資產負債表正常化 ，使安全資產供給
增加、稀缺性下降 ，進而推升實質利率 。疫情後實質利率上升
究竟是暫時現象 ，抑或代表新的長期均衡目前雖難預測 ，但從
全球儲蓄與投資平衡 的變化，地緣政治與氣候變遷等結構挑戰 ，
實質利率上升似乎是較有可能出現的情況。
利率短期維持高檔 ，待通膨與升息預期緩和才有回落空間： 能
源價格衝擊與通膨傳導將是牽動後續利率的主要因素 。歐央
2026年已升息二碼，存款機制利率上調至2.5%的中性區間上緣。

中東衝突持續下 ，歐央上調2027~2028年通膨預期 ，預計中期
內通膨率和核心通膨率將高於2%政策目標。目前天然氣期貨價
格已偏離歐央基準情境 、往不利情境靠攏 ，成品油價格因裂解
利差跳升而居高不下 ，因此，除非中東局勢迅速平息 ，否則預
估12月將再升一碼至 2.75%。9/28利率期貨反映 市場預期2027
年9月前將再升息超過3碼。不過，高能源價格若 延續較久，將
抑制經濟成長 ，殖利率上升也將發揮緊縮效果 、削弱通膨傳導 ，
我們預期歐央持續升息的必要性低於目前市場定價 。後續利率
上行空間將取決於能源價格與通膨是否進一步超出預期 ，待通
膨與升息預期緩和 ，利率將有回落空間 。惟考量結構性因素推
升，德國10年期公債殖利率預期將維持在3%以上；若要回落至
3%以下，可能需要看到歐央再次調降政策利率 ，預估4Q27才
有機會降息。

Q7：高利率對歐洲企業的影響？
首先，歐洲企業資產負債表整體維持健康 ，歐洲企業近年持續
去槓桿，淨債務/EBITDA比率維持相對低檔 ，整體財務槓桿不
高。而且，企業債務期限普遍較長 ，平均到期期限約五年 ，高
利率對企業利息負擔的傳導較為緩慢 ，因此，即使公債殖利率

上升，企業債務成本的重新定價將逐步反映 ，降低企業對利率
上升的敏感度。
另一方面 ，企業信貸需求仍具韌性 ，8月歐元區非金融企業貸
款年增4.2%，顯示截至目前企業融資活動並未因利率走高而明
顯降溫。此外，儘管歐洲公債殖利率大幅走高 ，歐洲投資級債
及非投資級債信用利差並未明顯擴大 ，顯示企業信用基本面 並
未出現明顯惡化。
整體而言，企業資產負債表維持健康 ，融資需求仍具韌性 ，信
用風險未明顯升高，高利率目前對企業的直接衝擊有限。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2005年以來至2026/9/23，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/9。

Q8：歐股展望？
2026年以來至9/28止，歐洲道瓊50指數上漲9%，過去一個月
出現回檔 ，能源價格再度上漲 、TTF天然氣期貨價格飆升至三
年多高點，央行立場偏鷹、公債殖利率大幅走高 ，加上歐洲政
治雜音增加，均對盤勢形成壓力 。然而，歐股自高點以來波段
回檔不到5%、幅度可控，企業獲利維持韌性扮演重要緩衝。
STOXX歐洲600企業2026年上半年每股盈餘較去年同期成長

14%，創近3年來最快增速 。雖然獲利成長主要由商品類股貢
獻，但商品以外類股上半年每股盈餘年增率亦上修至 7%。高
盛證券(2026/8)預估，2026年下半年歐洲企業獲利年增率將進
一步加速至19%，全年獲利預估成長15%。
短線歐股盤勢將受到油價與利率牽動 ，過去一個月德國十年期
公債殖利率跳升 36個基點 ，壓抑歐股評價 ，預估本益比降至
14.7倍(9/28)。目前美伊仍透過斡旋進行接觸 ，惟尚未看到達
成和解的明確路徑 ，在能源價格維持高檔 、通膨上行風險仍存
之下，歐央可能進一步升息，此外，2027年歐洲多國進入選舉
周期，政治不確定性將干擾市場情緒 ，令本益比擴張空間受限。
2027年企業獲利增速預估將由 2026年的高雙位數放緩至中個
位數，但仍維持穩定成長 ，將為股市提供基本面支撐 ，惟股市
動能預期將較為溫和。
Q9：高利率環境下，歐股布局聚焦哪些方向？
利率維持高檔、指數評價擴張空間有限下 ，歐股展望維持中立 ，
相對看好具獲利成長或政策支撐的產業 。銀行股受惠於目前利

率環境及企業信貸需求回升；工業 /國防、電氣化/能源安全及
AI等概念股則受惠於政策支持及相關投資需求增加。

銀行股受惠目前利率環境與信貸需求回升
銀行股獲利穩健，且在殖利率上升環境表現相對較佳 。歐洲央
行政策利率維持在 2%–3%區間，對銀行業有利 ；企業信貸需
求回升，有助銀行淨利息收入維持成長 ，摩根大通(2026/9)預
估，2026、2027年歐洲銀行NII將分別成長9%及6%。此外，
歐洲銀行CET1資本比率維持高檔 ，反映銀行具備充足的資本
緩衝。在上述因素支撐下 ，預估歐洲銀行2025~ 2028年提存

前營業利益年均複合成長8.8%，銀行獲利具韌性。
歐洲強化戰略自主，相關產業受益資本支出提升
歐洲強化戰略自主，工業/國防、電氣化/能源安全及AI等領域
受政策支持，將帶動相關投資需求 。隨著德國財政刺激逐步落
地，國防、基礎建設、能源及AI投資推進，有助支撐相關產業
的獲利動能。相較之下，能源價格維持高檔更久 ，對消費支出
構成風險，令消費股面臨逆風 ，需待地緣衝突緩解 。從獲利動
能來看，戰略自主受惠產業獲利動能持續優於消費類股 ，顯示
具政策與投資需求支撐的戰略產業獲利展望相對明確。

2026 年 10 月 19 日 全6 頁

中国：26 年 7～9 月は 4.5％成長の低空飛行 
さらなる景気テコ入れ策の可能性も

調査本部 主席研究員 齋藤 尚登

[要約] 
 中国国家統計局によると、2026 年7 月～9 月の実質GDP 成長率は前年同期比4.5％（以
下、断りのない限り変化率は前年比、前年同期比）となった。1 月～9 月は 4.6％と、
2026 年の政府成長率目標である 4.5％～5.0％のレンジ内となったが、 4 月～6 月
（4.3％）はこれを下回り、7 月～9 月は下限と同じだった。 
 当面の注目点は、2026 年10 月1 日に開始された新規住宅ローンに対する1％pt の財政
による利子補填で、住宅需要がどれほど刺激されるかである。予算がどの程度確保さ
れているのかは不明であるが、住宅購入は刺激されるとみられ、少なくとも当面の景
気をある程度下支えする可能性がある。それでも 2026 年の政府成長率目標である
4.5％～5.0％の下限の達成が危ぶまれる場合は、追加の景気テコ入れ策が打たれるこ
とになろう。大和総研では、預金準備率の引き下げにより、銀行の国債・地方債の保
有余力を高め、これらの増発によって景気テコ入れ策を強化することが有効だとみて
いる。

### Page 2

1

2 / 6

2026 年 7 月～9 月の実質 GDP 成長率は前年同期比 4.5％と低空飛行が続く 
中国国家統計局によると、2026 年 7 月～9 月の実質 GDP 成長率は前年同期比 4.5％（以下、
断りのない限り変化率は前年比、前年同期比）となった。1 月～9 月は4.6％と、2026 年の政府
成長率目標である 4.5％～5.0％のレンジ内となったが、4 月～6 月（4.3％）はこれを下回り、
7 月～9 月は下限と同じだった。 
2026 年1 月～9 月の4.6％成長に対する需要項目別寄与度は現時点では発表されていないが、
1 月～6 月との比較では内需が減速した一方で、7 月～9 月の貿易黒字の急拡大を受けて、純輸
出は寄与度を高めた可能性が高い。

消費は低迷。耐久消費財は需要先食いのツケを払う 
2026 年 7 月～9 月の小売売上は 0.7％増となり、4 月～6 月の 0.2％増から伸び率はやや加速
したものの低空飛行となった。1 月～9 月は 1.1％増であった。6 年目に突入した不動産不況に
よる逆資産効果に加え、自動車の買い替え補助金政策の効果一巡・車両購入税の徴収開始（一
貫して免税→価格の5％）の影響が色濃く現れている。 
2026 年 7 月～9 月の自動車販売金額は20.6％減となり、1 月～3 月の 9.1％減、4 月～6 月の
17.5％減からマイナス幅を一段と広げた。1 月～9 月は14.9％減であった。2024 年7 月25 日に
発表された「大規模設備更新と消費財買い替えへの支援強化に関する若干の措置」では、ガソ
リン車の補助金は従来の7,000 元から1.5 万元に、NEV（新エネルギー車）は1 万元から2 万元
に増額され、この政策は 2025 年も維持された。ただし、自動車販売台数で見たこの効果は、
2025 年秋までに一巡した。2026 年の補助金は、ガソリン車は価格の10％（上限は1.5 万 元 ）、

### Page 3

1

3 / 6

NEV は同12％（上限は2 万元）と定率補助が導入されている。さらに、 「内巻」と呼ばれる破滅
的な価格競争が続いているほか、2026 年からの NEV の車両購入税の徴収開始が響いている。こ
れは事前に発表されており、購入者の多くは2025 年末までに前倒しで購入した可能性が高い。 
なお、中国国家統計局によると、2026 年 1 月～9 月の小売売上は 1.1％増となったが、自動
車販売を除くと 2.8％増であった。自動車販売だけで小売売上の増加率を 1.7％pt 押し下げた
ことになる。買い替え補助金政策の効果一巡にせよ、車両購入税徴収開始前の駆け込み需要の
反動にせよ、需要の先食い政策のツケは大きいということだ。

2026 年 1 月～9 月の固定資産投資は 7.2％減に落ち込む 
2026 年 1 月～9 月の固定資産投資は7.2％減となり、1 月～6 月の 5.7％減からマイナス幅を
広げた。2025 年は 1989 年以来の減少（3.8％減）を記録したが、足元はそれを上回る減少幅と
なっている。分野別に、1 月～9 月の製造業投資は 2.1％減（1 月～6 月は 1.2％減） 、インフラ
投資は4.2％減（同2.4％減） 、不動産開発投資は20.6％減（同18.0％減）と、いずれもマイナ
ス幅を広げた。中でも不動産開発投資は 2022 年～2025 年にかけて年平均 13.5％減となった上
でのマイナス幅拡大であり、状況は極めて厳しい。 
2026 年1 月～9 月の不動産開発投資を除く固定資産投資は4.2％減となり、1 月～6 月の2.7％
減から一段と悪化した。これについても不動産不況の影響が大きい。具体的には、①中国では
資産運用を目的に住宅に投資する企業が多く、こうした企業もバランスシート調整の影響で投
資余力が低下している、②地方政府の主要な財政収入である土地使用権売却収入が大きく減少
し、地方政府の投資余力も低下している、ことなどによる。

1月～ 6月 2.7％減
1月～ 9月 4.2％減

### Page 4

1

4 / 6

製造業投資には、明るい材料もある。2026年1 月～9 月の高技術産業投資は4.9％増となり、
中でも情報サービス業は20.2％増、航空・宇宙関連機器・設備製造業は12.5％増を記録してい
る。いずれも新世代情報技術、航空・宇宙といった「戦略的新興産業」として、第15 次5 カ年
計画（2026 年～2030 年）でも中国共産党・政府が特に重視している産業だ。しかし、全体とし
て振るわないのは、内需が低迷する中で、供給過剰に苦しむ従来型製造業が 投資の抑制を余儀
なくしているためだ。 
インフラ投資は 2026 年 1 月～3 月こそ資金の一部となる地方政府特別債券の前倒し発行が功
を奏したが、2026 年の同債券の発行枠は 2025 年と同額の 4.4 兆元であり、既に息切れした格
好である。

貿易黒字が急増 
中国通関統計によると、2026 年 7 月～9 月の輸出（以下、貿易は米ドル建て）は28.4％増と
なり、1 月～3 月の14.7％増、4 月～6 月の20.1％増から一段と加速した。1 月～9 月は21.3％
増であった。ただし、これは原油・原材料価格上昇の一部が輸出価格に転嫁されたことや、世
界的なAI ブームを背景とした関連財の価格高騰によるところも大きい。 
品目別には、集積回路が倍増となり、コンピュータ・同部品の輸出も大きく伸びた。このほ
か、国内需要の低迷で輸出ドライブがかかる自動車の輸出も急増した。 
国・地域別には、最大の輸出先である ASEAN 向けが期を追うごとに加速したほか、米中貿易
戦争の休戦を受けて、米国向けも4 月～6 月以降、急増している。 
2026 年 7 月～9 月の輸入は 29.0％増となり、1 月～3 月の 23.6％増、4 月～6 月の 29.4％増
と同様の高水準の伸び率が続いた。1 月～9 月は27.5％増だった。内需は低迷しており、4 月～
6 月以降の輸入増加率のほとんどは輸入価格の上昇によるものである。 
品目別には、コンピュータ・同部品、集積回路の輸入が急増したほか、銅関連の輸入も大き
く増えた。銅関連については、大量の電力を消費するデータセンターの増設や電気自動車（EV）
の普及などにより送配電関連設備の需要が高まっていることが背景のひとつに挙げられよう。 
国・地域別には、中国とのサプライチェーンが構築されている ASEAN からの輸入伸び率が加
速し、4 月～6 月以降は米国からの輸入も急回復している。日本からの輸入も好調だ。一方、7
月～9 月の EU からの輸入は±0.0％と冴えない。中国と EU の貿易摩擦問題（中国の大幅輸出超）
が大きく影響している。 
2026 年 7 月～9 月の貿易黒字は 27.0％増の 3,645.5 億ドルとなり、1 月～3 月の 4.4％減、4
月～6 月の0.8％増から急増した。1 月～9 月の貿易黒字は7.8％増の9,381.1 億ドルとなった。
2025 年は 1.2 兆ドルの貿易黒字を計上し、欧米などから強く批判されたが、今年はそれを上回
る可能性がある。

### Page 5

1

5 / 6

図表 3 輸出入(前年同期比)と貿易収支(金額)の推移(単位：％、億ドル)

特に EU との間では、中国からのハイブリッド車（HV）やプラグインハイブリッド車（PHV）
の EU の輸入急増が問題視されていたが、両者は中国からのHV とPHV の輸入削減で合意した。
欧州委員会は2026 年10 月、4 年間の緊急輸入制限（セーフガード）を発動し、2026 年の実績
見込みの半分以下となる年間35 万台の輸入枠を設定し、この枠を毎年6％拡大するとした。中
国商務省の発表によると、EU が2025 年に中国から輸入したHV とPHV は合計30.2 万台であ
り、それを段階的に増やすと説明しているが、2026 年に輸入台数は急増し、80 万台程度にな
ると見込まれている。これを基にすれば、輸入台数は半減以下になる計算だ。 
さらに、中国財政部と中国国家税務総局は2026 年 10 月 12 日、NEV の付加価値税の輸出還付
率を現行の 13％から 9％に引き下げ、2027 年 4 月 1 日より実施するとした。全世界向けのNEV
の輸出が対象であり、輸出インセンティブは低下することになる。大和総研では、実施までの
間に一段の輸出ドライブがかかることを懸念している。

当面の注目点は住宅ローン金利 1％pt 分の利子補填による住宅需要刺激効果 
 最後に、当面の注目点は、2026 年 10 月 1 日に開始された新規住宅ローンに対する1％pt の
財政による利子補填で、住宅需要がどれほど刺激されるかである。対象は家計が取得する 1 軒
目の住宅で、面積 120 ㎡以下、価格 150 万元（1 元＝23.6 円で換算。約 3,540 万円）以下の物
件については、住宅ローンのうち最大100 万元を対象に、住宅ローン金利の1％pt 分が 5 年に
わたり財政から補給される。単純計算すると、5 年で 5 万元弱の利子補給が行われる計算だ。
予算がどの程度確保されているのか不明であるが、住宅購入は刺激されるとみられ、少なくと
も当面の景気をある程度下支えする可能性がある。

それでも 2026 年の政府成長率目標である 4.5％～5.0％の下限の達成が危ぶまれる場合は、
追加の景気テコ入れ策が打たれることになろう。大和総研では、預金準備率の引き下げにより、
銀行の国債・地方債の保有余力を高め、これらの増発によって景気テコ入れ策を強化すること
が有効だとみている。

Document 566: 内外経済ウォッチ『日本～米国の「リフレ脱却」要請と日米経済政策の実態～』(2026年11月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/内外経済ウォッチ『日本～米国の「リフレ脱却」要請と日米経済政策の実態～』(2026年11月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

日本
～米国の 「リフレ脱却」 要請と日米経済政策の実態～
2026年9月、 米国のスコ ッ ト ・ ベッセン ト財務長官が日
本政府および日本銀行に対し、 「アベノミクス的リフレ政
策からの脱却」 および 「利上げ ・ 積極財政の抑制」 を要請し
た発言は、 日米の金融 ・ 財政当局に大きな波紋を広げた。
しかし、 日米両国の財政赤字 ・ 金利体系 ・ インフレ動向 ・
通貨価値の変遷を客観的に比較 ・ 分析すると、 「リフレ政
策」 を真に推進しているのは日本ではなく、 むしろ米国自
身であるという逆説的な構造が浮き彫りとなる。
2026年9月初旬、 ベッセン ト米財務長官はＧ20等の機
会を通じて 「日本はアベノミクス期の過度なリフレ政策を
終えるべきだ」 と指摘し、 日銀による利上げ推進や政府の
財政拡大抑制を遠回しに迫った。
米国が日本にリフレ脱却を求める表面的な理由は 「円
安是正とインフレ抑制」 だが、 実質的には日本の利回り上
昇に伴い、 日本勢の資金が米債市場から逃避し、 米長期

金利が跳ね上がる事態を恐れている側面を指摘する向
きもある。 しかし、 「骨太シ ョ ッ ク」 と言われた骨太原案が公
表された6月30日以降のG7諸国の長期金利の動向を確
認すると、 最も上昇幅が少ないのは日本となっている。
① 「財政赤字」 による需要刺激
米国はパンデミ ッ ク以降も、 IRA （インフレ削減法） や
CHIPS法、 そして減税策の恒久化などを背景に、 景気拡
大局面にもかかわらず対GDP比6％超という戦時並みの
財政赤字を続けている。 これこそがリフレ政策の本質で
ある 「政府が借金をして民間に需要を創出する仕組み」 そ
はじめに
ベッセン ト発言の背景と波紋
なぜ米国のほうが 「リフレ推進度合いが
強い」 と言えるのか ？
のものである。 一方、 日本の財政赤字／GDP比は2025
年時点でG7諸国最小水準となっている。
② 純債務残高／GDP比の悪化
米国の一般政府純債務残高対GDP比は100％に近づ
いており、 上昇 トレン ドとなっている。 AIブームと トランプ

減税が相まって、 国内の資金需給がひっ迫しており、 米長
期金利の上昇が構造的な財政悪化をもたら している。 対
して日本の一般政府純債務残高対GDP比は、 インフレ経
済が定着した2023年以降に急速に低下しており、 2025
年時点では80％を下回っている。
③日本の金融政策正常化との対比
日本側はアベノミクス期の異次元緩和から離脱し、 日
銀は政策金利を段階的に引き上げ、 保有国債残高も年間
40～50兆円ペースで縮小させている。 すなわち、 日本が
「リフレ出口」 に向かって足早に動いている一方で、 米国は
伝統的金融政策面では利上げスタンスにシフ ト しつつ、
量的金融政策面ではバランスシー トの縮小をス ト ップし
ているのが実態である。
ベッセン ト財務長官による日本への 「リフレをやめよ」 と
いう言明は、 自国の財政膨張が招いている米長期金利の
上昇や ドル高 ・ 円安の歪みを、 他国の政策変更によって補
正しようとする 「責任転嫁のレ ト リ ッ ク」 という側面を拭え

ない。 本当にグローバル経済の安定化を目指すのであれ
ば、 批判されるべきは日本の金融 ・ 財政政策というより
も、 景気回復期にあっても巨額の財政赤字を続ける米国
自身の 「構造的リフレ依存」 であると言える。 日本政府 ・ 日
銀としては、 米国からの外圧に過剰に動揺することなく、
自国の自律的な賃金 ・ 物価循環に基づいた主体的かつ着
実な政策を推進することが求められる。
結論と示唆
経済調査部　首席エコノミス ト　永濱 利廣（ながはま としひろ）

十月 14, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  金融‧美國

JPMorgan Chase & Co.  
(JPM.N/JPM US)

3Q26 獲利低於預期，但 2027F 淨利息收入展望正向 
重要訊息  
摩根大通(JPM)3Q26 每股盈餘 5.58 元，低於市場及凱基預期 5.5%及 4.6%，
但上修 2026 淨利息收入及營業費用展望。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 獲利季減 28%(扣除一次性季減 10%) 、年增 6%。JPM 3Q26 稅後淨
利 153 億美元， 為今年單季低點， 主因 2Q26 一次性處分利益 42 億元拉高
基期，且 3Q26 資本市場疲弱造成非利息收入季減 27%(自營與造市交易損
益季減 28%、投行手續費 收入季減 8%及認列 5 億美元債券虧損) ，抵銷放
款、資產管理及信用卡收入季增 3%、4%及 6%。不過 3Q26 核心獲利 淨利
息收入( NII)達 270.4 億美元( 季增 6%、年增 13%)，略優於預期 ，加上營業
費用持平 ，抵銷淨提存 季增 2 億元( 信用成本季增 4 基點)。 
3Q26 淨利差 回 升 、 資產品質持續改善。 3Q26 淨利差季 增 8 基點至
2.48%，其中存放利差季增 5 基點， 加上放款年增 10%、季增 3%，高於主
要銀行同業 7.5% 、1.4% 。企金與信用卡資產品質 持續改善，整體逾放比季
減 0.02% 至 0.59% 、呆帳 沖銷率連續 四季下滑 至 0.59%，其中信用卡沖銷
率下降 0.43 百分點至 2.9% ，創 4Q23 以來新低， 並低於公司今年預估之
3.2%。在資本管理上，公司調高每股現金股利至 1.65 美元，但策略性縮減
季度股票回購至 10 億美元( 僅佔 500 億美元計畫 2%)，以保留資本投入報
酬更具吸引力 的業務成長機會 。3Q26 CET1 比率季減 30 基點至 13.9% ，除
配息及 RWA 成長 2%影響外 ，主要 AOCI 債券評價未實現虧損 78.9 億元。 
2027F 淨利息收入 展望正向。 管理層小幅上修 2026F 淨利息收入 0.7% 至 
1,062 億美元(年增 11%)，因持續投資， 將 2026F 營業費用微幅 0.1% 至 
1,086 億美元( 年增 12%)，但消金資產品質保持穩定，維持全年信用卡淨沖
銷率 3.2% 。管理層 初步預估 2027F 排除市場交易之淨利息收入為 1,070 億
美元(年增 10%)，其中 25%增幅來自預估 未來 3 次各 25 基點升息之殖利
率曲線上揚，其餘 75% 來自企金與信用卡放款成長帶動資產負債表擴大，
以及較低 存款 Beta 值預期，並初估 2027F 營業費用為市場共識之 1,140 億
美元( 年增 5%)。管理層表示 JPM 已管控 AI 放款集中度風險，認為相關直接
信用曝險仍相對有限，近期已收緊對高槓桿企業授信，包括 PIK 貸款、弱化
授信契約 (Weak Covenants) 及債權順位弱化 (Priming )等放款， 採取審慎防禦
性授信策略。  
投資建議  
由於 2H26 資本市場波動影響 2026F 獲利成長動能趨緩，加上聯準會重啟升
息循環提高資產品質不確定性，凱基維持摩根大通「持有」投資建議， 目標
價 375 美元(約 2026F -27F 每股盈餘 15 倍)；惟若股價因市場波動回檔至  
320 美元以下，考量評價修正且公司可能再度增加股票回購，建議投資人可
逢低適度布局 2027F 升息循環尾聲之基本面及淨值改善題材。  
投資風險  
美國失業 率增加、高利率持續 影響銀行資產品質惡化； 全球金融市場轉弱。

圖 14：公司概況  圖 15：JPM 組織架構圖 
摩根大通（ JPMorgan Chase & Co. ）成立於 1871 年，總部位
於美國紐約 ，業務涵蓋投資銀行、商業銀行、資產管理和財富
管理。公司於 2000 年由摩根大通與大通銀行合併而成 ，在全
球 100 多個國家提供金融服務。摩根大通在華爾街擁有強大
影響力，為企業、政府和個人提供投資、貸款、支付和風險管
理等服務。其品牌包括「 J.P. Morgan 」和「Chase 」，前者主
要負責投資銀行和資產管理業務，後者則專注於個人及商業銀
行業務。作為美國最大銀行之一，摩根大通在全球擁有超過  
31.7 萬名員工， 4Q25 資產規模 達 4.42 兆美元。

Document 589: 凱基投顧_半導體產業_潘俊宏_20261014

### Page 1

十月 14, 2026 研究網站:  https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

產業報告  台灣

半導體產業

封測產業：資本支出與擴產規模創歷史新高  
重要訊息 
台灣封測廠 2026-27 年資本支出與擴產規模將創歷史新高。  
評論及分析  
數十年難得一見的成長機會。台灣前八大封測廠年初迄今已上修 2026 年資本
支出 45.9%至 4,839 億元，創歷史新高，較 2025 年幾乎翻倍(年增 99%，相
較過去 10 年複合年成長率 15.4% )。我們認為大幅擴產主要反映：( 1) AI 晶
片出貨強勁帶動既有業務成長；( 2)晶片尺寸擴大及設計複雜度提高，將 消耗
更多產能；(3)台積電(2330 TT ，NT$2,490，增加持股)將資源重新配置至先進
製程與先進封裝，並逐步退出晶圓測試(CP) 等毛利率較低或非核心業務。由
於上述成長動能均屬結構性因素，我們預期封測廠未來兩年資本支出仍將維
持高檔。  
先進封裝與晶圓測試需求龐大 。目前我們擁有的資訊仍不足以精確估算 AI 晶
片 CP 測試市場規模。然而，根據我們的初步估算及供應鏈調查，AI 晶片 CP
測試營收規模預估 達 500-600 億元，且將隨測試時間增加而持續成長。在假
設每投入 1 元資本支出可創造 0.5 元營收的情境下，我們預估 CP 測試所需資
本支出將超過 1,000 億元，占 2026 年封測廠整體資本支出 20-25%。我們預
估台灣封測廠資本密集度將由 2025 年的 28.9%升至 2026 年的 44.1%  (過去
10 年平均為 16.6% )，部分公司甚至超過 100%，遠高於半導體產業平均的
15.5%  (記憶體產業 2026 年營收預期成長超過 280%，拉低整體產業平均資
本密集度)。 
大規模擴產將加重財務負擔 。在封測廠中，以測試業務為主的公司因資本密
集度較高，故面臨的財務壓力可能更大，包括京元電子 (2449 TT，NT$296.5，
增加持股；資本密集度 104%)、欣銓(3264 T T，NT$278，未評等； 62%)及
台星科 (3265 TT ，NT$292.5，未評等；235%；其雖以封裝業務為主，但新
增產能主要用於 CP 測試)。而日月光投控(3711 TT ，NT$747，增加持股)整體
資本密集度相對同業較低，但若僅考量 AI 業務營收及相關擴產，資本密集度
亦超過 100%。我們認為封測廠資本支出仍 具進一步上修空間，因此多數業
者 2026- 27年將面臨龐大的資金需求 。 例如 ， 京元電子近期宣布與NVIDIA (美)
簽訂客戶預付款協議，以支應美國產能擴充。 NVIDIA 亦與 Amkor  (美)簽訂價
值 15 億美元的協議，透過預付款及多年期策略生產合作支持其擴產。我們預
期後續將有更多封測廠會 透過海外可轉債、聯貸、現金增資及可轉債來進行
籌資，預估 2027 年封測廠折舊費用將年增 30-100%。 
投資建議  
我們預期封測廠積極擴大資本支出與產能將有利於：(1) 設備供應商，包括
Advantest( 日)、鴻勁 (7769 TT，NT$5,780，增加持股) 及致茂(2360 TT ，
NT$2,250，未評等)；(2)測試介面供應商，包括中華精測 (6510 TT，NT$3,225 , 
增加持股)、穎崴(6515 TT，NT$5,615，增加持股)及旺矽(6223 TT，NT$5,885，
增加持股 )。封測族群中，我們看好 CPU 業務占比較高的日月光投控， 係因
NVIDIA V era Rubin 及 AMD (美) Venice 均將帶動其 CoWoS 全製程業務成長 ，
而台積電 2027-28 年持續擴產亦將為其載板端業務帶來成長空間 。 
投資風險  
若 AI 需求放緩，資本密集度及折舊費用較高 下，封測廠面臨的財務壓力將高
於其他半導體次產業。  
焦點內容

1. 數十年難得一見的成長機會。  
2. 先進封裝與晶圓測試需求龐大。  
3. 大規模擴產將加重財務負擔。

凱基投顧

股價說明 等級 定義 
 增加持股 (OP) 對個股持正面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現超越凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 持有 (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未 來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 降低持股 (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 未評等 (NR) 凱基證券未對該個股加以評等。  
 受法規限制 
未評等 (R) 
受凱基證券集團內部政策和 /或相關法令限制使凱基證券集團無法進行某些形式的資訊交流，其
中包括提供評等給投資人參考。

聯準會升息了，投等債還能買嗎？ 
 9 月聯準會升息一碼，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息，雖年內尚具升息空間，但
資金流仍持續流入美國投等債，顯示市況紛擾無損其資金避風港的特質。

出刊日：2026/10/8 
222 期

### Page 2

2026

02-8751-6665 
 www.fubon.com 
台北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號 
固本收利

本期內容： 
⚫ 經濟數據、市場供
需等持續干擾債市 
⚫ 美國投等債仍為資
金良好避風港 
⚫ 建議逢利率回彈時
分批佈局投等債

重點摘要

■ 為因應頑固的通膨壓力 ，9 月聯準會升
息 1 碼，預期年內至少再升息 1 碼。
第 3 季美國投等債價格承壓是受美國
數據強勁、大量投等債發債、通膨預期
加上聯準會鷹派訊號等因素影響。

■ 總體經濟強韌提供絕佳的信用支撐，
高評級公司違約風險或信用降評機率
微乎其微。今年以來美國投資等級債
維持淨流入， 顯示市場動盪時美國投
等債仍為資金避風港。

■ 目前美國投等債殖利率 處於近年相對
較高的位置，投資人應以息收為主要
考量，建議投資人把握殖利率回彈時
機，分批進行佈局鎖定高債息收益。

▋ 聯準會 3 年多來首度升息 
9 月聯準會票委全數贊成通過升息 1 碼，將
聯邦資金利率目標調升為 3.75%至 4.00%
區間，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息。美國
經濟維持穩健擴張 ，聯準會對於 2026、
2027 年經濟成長預期均上修 0.1 個百分點
至 2.3%、2.4%，就業供需平穩且失業率穩
定，2026、2027 年失業率預期均下修 0.2
個百分點至 4.1%，會後聲明新增國內支出
具韌性描述。今年 PCE 和核心 PCE 均上修
0.1 個百分點至 3.7%和 3.4%，9 月聯準會
升息是為因應頑固的通膨壓力，18 名與會
官員中有 16 位委員預期今年內至少再升息
1 碼，明年聯準會維持 6 月的通膨預期， 政
策利率亦維持不變。(圖二、圖三)

圖二：美國經濟穩健，通膨具僵固性

▋ 殖利率飆壓抑 Q3 投等債價格 
第 3 季美國投等債價格下跌，是今年最差
單季表現 ， 相比去年第3 季以及過去 30 年
第 3 季的投等債價格表現亦相對遜色。這
是受到近期美國就業 、 生產數據表現強勁 ，
今年前 8 個月科技相關投等債發債量已超
越去年全年表現 ， 中東戰爭持續支撐國際油
價在高位 ， 而居高不下的通膨預期持續推升
美債殖利率走勢等 ， 加上聯準會主席Warsh
自 6 月主持個人首場利率會議起，即反覆
強調聯準會穩定物價的鷹派訊號， 持續為投
等債走勢構成壓力。(圖四、圖五)

▋ 那投資等級債還能買嗎？ 
美國經濟數據強勁意味在強韌的總體經濟
下 ， 提供絕佳的信用支撐 ， 即便面臨高利率
環境 ， 大型龍頭企業營運有望維持穩健 ， 加
上企業帳上現金充裕 ， 投資等級高的公司違
約風險或信用降評的機率將微乎其微。

隨著各國債券殖利率攀升 ，加劇全球風險資
產高檔震盪 ， 市場避險情緒明顯升溫， 今年
以來美國投資等級債維持淨流入， 根據小摩
數據顯示，至 9/24 流入投等債資金已直逼
2025 年，相比非投等債和新興市場債僅達
去年約一半的水準，顯示在市場動盪時 ， 美
國投資等級債的高流動性和 極佳避險韌性
仍為資金提供避風港而持續吸金。(圖六)

圖六：目前流入投等債資金直逼去年水準 
資料來源：JPM，2026/9/24

▋ 殖利率達近年高點具吸引力 
目前美國投等債利差落在近年低點 ， 相對較
無收斂空間 ， 但殖利率則落在5%至 6%間，
處於近年相對較高的位置 ， 高殖利具有投資
吸引力 ， 投資人應以息收為主要考量 ， 目前
包含金融公司、通訊產業債殖利率在6%以
上 ， 為殖利率相對領先的產業族群 ， 建議在
當前升息預期濃厚下 ， 投資人應把握殖利率
回彈的時機 ， 分批進行佈局以鎖定高債息收
益。(圖七、圖八)

圖七：目前投等債以息收為主 
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9/24

圖八：目前金融 、 科技等投等債殖利率較高 
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9/24

市場風險緩解，股市勁揚
投

略
市場展望投資策略
 承作中天期投等金融機構債：美股第二季企業獲利普遍
優於華爾街預期，整體獲利年增率創5年新高，3Q26財
報季將開始，企業獲利成長預估支撐信用利差維持低檔。
金融機構債目前相對估值便宜，利差較其他產業債具收
斂空間，預期後市表現佳。(詳見Page 12、13)
 逢回加碼科技股：全球利率走升成為短期壓抑科技股的
關鍵，然而參考過去升息週期科技股報酬正向，且近期
新AI硬體新技術突破可望在未來AI伺服器布局。(詳見
Page 15、16)
 中東風險猶存，能源股成為最佳避險標的：美伊談判不
如預期樂觀，油價重新上漲，長期而言能源類股為高油
價及升息週期受惠的資產，可善用相關ETF做短線操作。
(詳見Page 18)
 匯市展望：歐元在疲軟的消費者信心數據後走低 ，而上
週日本休市三日 ，交易清淡 ，日本重新開市後資金重新
湧入放大短期波動 ，預期USDJPY維持在 155~160區間
波動，市場對 Fed短期內升息的預期升溫 ，預估美元指

數強勢走揚，本週美元指數區間看在99.50~102.5。
 債市展望：全球央行利率會議告一段落 ，相關消息反應
出盡，波動幅度明顯收窄 ，對未來升息預期定價明朗 。
標售不如預期，預估債市表現承壓 ，本週十年美債殖利
率區間看在5.10%~5.25%。
 股市展望: 即使Anthropic執行長呼籲放緩AI前沿模型發
布導致震盪，上週又再次更新前沿模型，Meta Muse使
用人數暴增，AI需求強勁，半導體類股領漲 ，AI應用前
景明朗 ，股市展望正向 ，本週標普 500指數區間看在
7,600~7,750。

頭條新聞
1. 美中元首在白宮舉行川習會 ，雙方達成八點成果共識 。
主要成果包括貿易休戰協議延長至 2027年1月10日，並
達成300億美元對等降稅安排 ，超過九成產品免除相互
加徵的全部關稅 。此外，雙方正式同意建立中美 AI對話
機制，並就禁毒執法及國際水道通行等議題達成合作。
2. 芝加哥聯準銀行總裁古斯比指出 ，若通膨源於反覆出現
的供給衝擊，央行仍難逃在 「就業」與「物價」間抉擇
並被迫緊縮，9/24美國財政部標售的5年期與2年期公債
買氣普遍疲軟，得標殖利率顯著走高 ，市場對供應增加
與通膨黏性疑慮升溫，美債殖利率面臨沈重上行壓力。
3. 美國與伊朗在聯合國大會場邊舉行間接談判期間 ，伊朗
提出重新開放荷姆茲海峽 ，但遭美方拒絕 。美國談判代
表表示，德黑蘭並不控制這條水道 ，並質疑伊朗是否有
權提出這項條件。中東局勢再使油價飆升市場震盪。
產業訊息
1. Meta推出的個人AI代理Muse爆紅登頂App Store榜首，

下載量破250萬次並帶動股價飆漲。其運算需求點燃CPU
與硬體商機，受惠推論通用CPU概念股，AI AGENT概念
走強未必激勵AI訓練模型的尖端晶片需求。(詳見Page 6)
2. 德州AI資料中心快速建設推升水電費用引爆選前民怨 ，
排隊接電量達歷史峰值逾五倍且九成來自 AI，成為選戰
關鍵焦點 。選民強大壓力 ，州長艾波特 9/21強硬下令德
州環境品質委員會全面暫停核發環境許可 ，直到完成電
網與水資源稽核，並要求業者自負基建成本平息民憤。
3. 儘管呼籲 AI 模 型 發 展 暫 緩， 然而 Anthropic 隨即發表
Claude Opus 5.5，效能直逼Fable 5.1且成本大減，工作
負載成本比 Opus 5 低 40% ， 輸 出 速 度 也 快 逾30% ，
OpenAI則將GPT-6產品線Sol、Luna模型雙雙降價 50%。

台灣外銷訂單連19紅，8月金額創新高
5
台灣外銷訂單表現強勁優於預期 圖表解讀
➢ 台灣8月外銷訂單創三個新高 (單月、年增、累積)，單
月達1,029.6億美元，首度突破千億美元 ，月增5.1%、
年增71.4%，連續19個月正成長 。累計1至8月外銷訂
單達7,049.9億美元、年增55.0%。
➢ 8月訂單亦明顯優於統計處原先預估的 970億至990億
美元，主要反映 AI、高效能運算與雲端服務需求進一
步擴張 。除GPU等AI晶片需求維持強勁外 ，部分雲端
服務供應商加快發展自研 ASIC晶片，帶動國內 IC設計
業者相關訂單超乎預期 。資訊通信與電子產品接單合
計達799.6億美元，占整體訂單 77.7%；兩者較去年同
期合計增加380.2億美元，占8月年增金額約88.7%。
➢ 8月訂單再創新高仍高度集中於 AI伺服器、半導體與雲
端基礎建設供應鏈 ，台股今年股市強勁表現具強勁基
本面支撐。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9/23

### Page 6

META MUSE引爆AI代理對CPU的需求
6
META及CPU概念股近日走強 圖表解讀
➢ 先前市場認為 Meta的開源模型商業策略不足以和閉源
模型抗衡，然而，AI AGENT重點來自於模型能力與用
戶基礎，Meta藉由Muse Video與Muse系列多模態代
理模型落地，模型能力擠進前三，結合Instagram龐大
社群生態 ，成功將生成式 AI轉化為高黏著度的商業應
用，扭轉了市場對其開源生態變現力不足的疑慮。
➢ AI AGENT不同於GPU，反而需龐大的CPU，所以美國
CPU概念股走強 ，推論需求並不完全等同於前沿訓練 。
更有可能是下一段行情不單單是少數幾家實驗室砸下
數兆美元 、打造愈來愈龐大的運算叢集 。也許會是數
十億個分散各處的代理 ，這並不會扼殺前沿領域的行
情，而是拓寬AI基礎設施需求的面貌。
➢ 亞馬遜因隱私與安全疑慮 ，封鎖Muse進入購物網站，
Shopify則與Meta合作，開放AI代理人結帳。Muse能
不能普及，也取決於各家網站願不願意開放。
資料來源：Google Finance(上圖) 2026/9/22, TrendForce(下圖) 2026/7

### Page 7

澳預計維持利率，聚焦美PCE及美光財報
7
重點提醒
➢ 本週焦點為美國 8月核心PCE、9月非農就業及澳洲央行
的利率決議。緊縮貨幣政策應對澳洲 居高不下的通膨壓
力，預期澳洲央行將維持官方現金利率於 4.35%不變。
Fed於9/16如期升息 1碼，今年底前仍有再一次升息的
空間，向市場釋放出強烈的鷹派訊號。
➢ Fed維持偏鷹立場 ，市場將高度檢視 9/30公布的美國核
心PCE物價指數 ，以及10/2的非農就業人數 。通膨指標
能持續穩定 ，且就業市場未現大幅疲軟 ，有助市場消化
高利率環境緩解對經濟衰退的擔憂。
➢ 財報重點在10/1布記憶體大廠美 光，儘管近期遭遇亞洲
罷工，預期美光營收達508億美元，年增近350%。輝達
財務長前次財報表示 ，輝達AI伺服器面臨 「極端的記憶
體價格環境 」，並表示「記憶體價格漲幅已超出我們先
前預期，而且明年還會進一步走高」階露記憶體短缺。

### Page 8

升息激勵美元轉強，主要貨幣及黃金下跌
8
匯市表現說明
➢ 美元指數：聯準會於9月維持貨幣緊縮政策及高利率環境
的預期推動下 ，美元持續走強 ，受惠於相對較高的殖利
率優勢，美元上週表現強勢，近期匯市的最強勢資產。
➢ 黃金： 受到美元走強 、市場對各國債務壓力的潛在擔憂
與全球多數央行升息預期升溫的多重夾擊 ，短期金價走
勢承受重壓。
➢ WTI原油：油價上週一度承壓，主要原因是波斯灣原油供
應逐步增加 ，加上市場期待美國與伊朗可能透過外交途
徑結束衝突 。然而週四伊朗總統佩澤希齊揚在聯合國大
會表示德黑蘭永不投降 ，重新推升市場對中東衝突延續
及原油供應風險的擔憂再轉強。

### Page 9

升息不如預期鷹派，日圓續貶後區間震盪
9
日本外匯干預金額屢創新高 圖表解讀
➢ 日銀總裁植田和男對 升息未如預期鷹派 ，日圓不僅未
能止跌 ，跌勢反倒擴大 ，被認為植田的說法未讓投資
者理解及滿意 ，日銀的基本立場並未改變 ，市場仍維
持每3個月升息一次，仍定價對12月升息的預期。
➢ 日本連休期間交易清淡 ，美元兌日圓波動且缺乏明確
方向。先前日圓貶勢擴大，而截至8月26日的一個月內，
日本干預匯市支出達創歷史紀錄的 15.4兆日圓 ，約近
千億美元 ，不排除在日圓繼續貶值後進一步干預 ，預
計在下週開盤匯率波動放大。
➢ USDJPY若達160則建議站在賣方 ，USDJPY2年期遠期
點數已逼近7月新低，預計未來日銀鷹派政策正常化帶
動國內殖利率上升 、日本資產吸引機構資金回流 ，以
及AI相關投資增加等三大結構性因素 ，日圓中長期走
勢將逐漸獲得支撐，大幅續貶可能性較低。

### Page 10

股市情緒修復迅速，美元震盪走高
10
避險情緒維持低位，美元指數走升 圖表解讀
➢ 市場政策不確定性消除 ，股市避險情緒迅速降溫 ，VIX
指數已快速回落至 20以下的相對低位 。儘管美聯儲重
啟了自 2023年7月以來的首次升息 ，美元指數並未出
現單邊強勢，而是維持在100上下呈現區間震盪。這顯
示高達86%機率的升息預期早已被市場所充分定價 ，
資金流動性正在重新尋求平衡。
➢ 受歐元區9月綜合PMI意外大幅萎縮至 48.9重返景氣收
縮區間衝擊 ，歐洲央行10月降息預期驟升 ，帶動10年
期德債殖利率挫跌並促使美德利差走闊至 1.5% 以上；
加上歐元兌美元自 1.16 高位回落跌破 1.15，技術面與
利差基本面同步轉向支撐美元 ，短線分析預估歐元反
彈乏力 ，美元指數在美德利差擴大與歐美經濟基本面
分歧下將延續偏強震盪走勢。

### Page 11

債市隨升息表現壓抑
11
債市表現說明
➢ 美國公債：Fed理事巴爾表示，為使通膨順利回落至政策
目標，未來可能仍須進一步調整貨幣政策 。此外，5年期
美債標售需求疲弱 ，推升各期美債殖利率走高 ，其中10
年期美債殖利率週四盤中一度突破 5.13%，刷新2007年7
月以來新高紀錄。CME Fed Watch工具顯示，市場交易
員押注Fed於10月升息1碼的機率已攀升至 66%以上，相
較一個月前的8.8%更有顯著增長。
➢ 非投等債： 升息週期下債市表現轉弱 ，資金明顯轉向高
品質收益資產 ，隨著殖利率走高與企業融資成本增加的
擔憂，信用債市場普遍受挫。
➢ 新興市場當地債： 聯準會重啟升息引發國際資金轉趨保
守，在強勢美元與高位美債殖利率的雙重夾擊下 ，新興
市場資產大幅承壓 。整體國際投資氛圍的不佳 ，抵銷部
分新興國家國內的寬鬆利多 ，持續牽制新興當地債走勢 。

### Page 12

利率攀高，投等債基金獲資金湧入
12
投等債利率攀高
圖表解讀
➢ 美 國 投 等 債 企 業2Q 營 收 年 增10.5% 、 EBITDA 年增
15.8%，皆創近4年來最強表現 ，主要受惠大型科技企
業與AI投資、油價走高 ，以及醫療保健營收貢獻 ，總
槓桿率降至近3年低點，利息保障倍數維持穩定 ，企業
營運與償債能力仍具韌性，信用基本面仍相對穩健。
➢ 近期美國投等債殖利率隨美國公債殖利率攀升至 5.7%，
顯著高於 10年均值 3.98%，使收益水準回到近年相對
高檔，儘管今年以來美國 10年期公債殖利率震盪走高 ，
利率上行壓力再度升高 ，但年初以來國際資金仍持續
流入投等債基金。
➢ 從週度流量觀察 ，多數期間維持淨流入 ，且截至9/16
當週仍延續正流入 ，顯示利率上行與短線報酬波動尚
未明顯削弱投等債需求 ，資金仍傾向透過投等債鎖定
較高殖利率水準。
資料來源： Bloomberg, 2026/9/22(上圖)、JPM, 2026/9/24(下圖)

圖表解讀
➢ 過去10年投資級債券各產業的 OAS歷史估值比較矩陣 ，
金融機構板塊展現出顯著的相對評價優勢 。矩陣數據
顯示，金融機構與基礎材料 、資本財 、科技、公用事
業等絕大多數產業相比 ，其相對估值多落在 90以上的
深綠色區間 。較高的數值代表目前金融債的利差水準
相較於其他產業 ，處於過去10年來相對寬闊 （便宜）
的位置 ，投資級債券內部具備較高的獲利與收斂空間 。
➢ 從標普金融產業來看 ，銀行、資本市場與金融服務這3
個次產業的獲利動能同樣延續 。2026年EPS年增率分
別達23%、19%與14%，其中銀行 、資本市場表現最
為突出 。從季度變化來看 ，銀行2QEPS年增率達 41%，
資本市場也達28%，上半年獲利動能明顯較強。
➢ 下半年成長率放緩，但銀行與資本市場3Q、4Q仍維持
雙位數成長 ，金融服務亦 延續正增長 ，顯示金融產業
獲利表現仍有延續性 ，也使金融機構債在投等債內部
更具觀察價值。

➢ 根據歷史過去5次聯準會首次啟動升息後的股價表現 ，

➢ 能源產業提供穩健報酬，唯一在1M到12M所有觀察期
內全數保持正報酬的板塊 。升息通常伴隨通膨攀升與
大宗商品需求旺盛，能源股具備極佳的抗通膨效果。
➢ 傳統觀念常認為資訊科技因升息表現不佳 ，然而升息
後12個月平均上漲 52%，前3個月雖微幅下跌 ，但第4
個月起迅速轉正 ，具備高毛利與定價權的優質成長股
能完全克服利率成本。

代人工的營運成本 ，可望激勵相關非必需股票獲利及
股價表現。

### Page 17

圖表解讀
➢ 美國煉油廠為因應高價柴油需求 ，大幅將產能轉向柴
油，導致2026年美國汽油產出率降至 54.9%，產出排
擠效應使得汽油供應快速收緊，同步帶動OECD汽柴油
商業庫存雙雙滑落至歷史季節性區間下緣 ，汽油庫存
顯著的年減 。成品油替代難度高與庫存耗減 ，促使原
油裂解價差維持高檔，支撐能源股漲勢核心動能。
➢ 能源兩大權值股埃克森美孚與雪佛龍煉油產品營收占
比皆超過 70%。在柴油享有龐大地緣政治溢價 ，且汽
油因供給緊縮而醞釀龐大上行空間的雙重利多下 ，煉
油毛利的持續擴張對其營運動能是極大的正面貢獻 ，
短期內仍將是市場對抗通膨與避險的焦點。
➢ 展望後市 ，煉油廠重柴油 、輕汽油的結構性傾斜正迅
速改變成品油的供需版圖 。若中東與俄烏地緣衝突進
一步壓抑全球煉油產出 ，2027年前汽柴油的整體利潤
不僅能維持高位 ，甚至具備進一步走高的潛力 ，為能
源類股提供強勁的獲利支撐與評價上調空間。

10月機會市場亮點
18
機
會
市

資產別 市場 亮點
股票
市場
美國 美國經濟仍維持溫和成長，企業獲利持續支撐股市表現。市場焦點由降息預期逐步轉向企業基本面與AI資本支出，雲端、半導體及電力基礎建設仍是主要受惠族群，惟高評價下美國波動放大。
新亞 台股處AI硬體與晶圓代工核心，輝達財報帶動台廠伺服器與AI供應鏈，加上台積電及AI供應鏈獲利持續向上，是台股支柱。HBM需求旺，DRAM價格飆，韓國是記憶體產業趨勢的最大受惠者。
日本 日企資本支出意向仍具韌性，並受到多項結構性因素支持，官方支持性政策、結構性再通膨及日圓疲弱，成為日股重要推動。日企盈利動力仍然強勁，而AI相關資本開支亦成為另一項催化因素。
科技 2027年前AI半導體仍處獲利成長速度快於估值擴張速度階段，維持對美國半導體與A 基礎建設鏈偏多看法。超大規模雲端服務商提供更清晰證據，AI支出正在轉化為實際收入，支撐獲利預期增強。
非必須
消費
H1表現落後的汽車產業動能逐步回穩，預期Tesla 3Q毛利率可維持穩定，看好Robotaxi、自動駕

駛與人形機器人業務於2027年開始進入商業化擴張階段，有望帶動非必需消費產業重獲市場關注。
金融 美國利率仍維持相對高檔，有利銀行淨利息收入。資本市場活動回溫、IPO與企業併購增加，主要權值金融股評價高於歷史平均，預期金融股仍具防禦與收益優勢，但超額報酬空間可能有限。
原物料/
能源
美、歐及亞洲經濟穩，工業活動、運輸及資料中心用電需求增加，能源需求可望同步上升。OPEC
認為能源需求至2050年仍持續成長。實物商品物理性短缺，將推動貴金屬與基本金樹價格攀升。
債券
市場 投等債 美國投等債殖利率維持吸引力信用品質佳、違約率低，現階段重點在領取收益而非賺價差，美國大型銀行獲利穩定、資本充足率維持高水準，金融債提供高於一般企業債之收益率。
匯率
市場 美元 美國核心通膨降溫趨勢延續，但財政赤字及美債供給增加仍壓抑長端利率下降空間。美元指數短線可能維持區間震盪，第四季需留意美國預算協商及期中選舉相關政治風險對市場情緒的影響。

19
